## What the guide adds (scripts, keys, method)
- **Front matter (printed pp.2–25):** Scope and sequence; Introduction (7-level course, Pre-A1 to B1 CEFR); Course components; Unit walkthrough; Assessment (English Benchmark; formative assessment; analytic rubrics); How to… (projects, mixed-ability groups, stories, posters); Classroom language; Games bank (incl. a Repetition game).
- **Lesson notes (printed pp.26–143):** step-by-step notes for every Pupil's Book and Activity Book lesson with aims, target language, materials, procedures, **full audioscripts**, **answer keys**, Assessment for Learning boxes, GSE descriptors, and Diversity/Support notes. Each unit opens with a Mapping page (Jordan curriculum themes; e.g. Unit 2: Theme 1 Society, Theme 2 Culture; Unit 3: Theme 2 Culture, Theme 6 Recreation). Exam-style practice is built in: pages headed "Get ready for…" and "Pre A1 Starters Reading and Writing" parts prepare pupils for Cambridge Pre A1 Starters-style tasks.
- **Progress path (printed pp.142–143):** riddle/review pages ("When do you have dinner?"; "You wear it on your head, but it's not a hat. What is it?"; "We can ride on this. It's long. It doesn't go on a road.") before the glossary.
- **Glossary (printed pp.144–145):** all taught words with IPA, by unit.

## Full unit/lesson map (lesson notes)
- Welcome — Back to school: notes from p.26 (alphabet, numbers 11–20, demonstratives).
- Unit 1 — It's a happy day!: unit objectives p.35 ("to talk about daily routines and times of the day"); notes pp.35–56 (incl. Pre A1 Starters practice and a "Get ready for…" page).
- Unit 2 — Let's dress up!: unit objectives p.57 ("to talk about clothes"); notes pp.57–78.
- Unit 3 — The activity centre: unit objectives p.79 ("to talk about activities and days of the week"); notes pp.79–100.
- Unit 4 — I want to be a teacher!: unit objectives p.101 ("to talk about jobs and transport"); notes pp.101–121 (Unit 4's story is explicitly linked to the Social Science curriculum in the notes).
- Language booster 1: pp.122–125. Maths CLIL "How do we tell the time?": pp.126–129.
- Festivals: Months and seasons pp.130–133; **Calgary Stampede pp.134–137 (see negatives)**; Independence Day pp.138–141.
- Progress path pp.142–143; Glossary pp.144–145; Notes pages; credits.

## Grammar and vocabulary spine for the book
Taught spine as in the Pupil's Book Semester 1 (demonstratives; routines + present simple; wearing + possessives; third-person present simple; want to be + by transport; blends pl/bl, dr/tr/br, sk/sw/st, wh). The guide adds the assessment spine: GSE can-do statements per lesson and Pre A1 Starters-format practice.

## Honest negatives
- **Major identity surprise:** the guide contains a complete festival unit that does not exist in the Jordan Pupil's Book — **Festival 2, the Calgary Stampede** (Canada; cowboy, cowboy hat, cowboy boots, rodeo, race, tipis, craftwork, barbecue; project: make a cowboy hat), with reproduced Pupil's Book pages numbered 54–55 and full lesson notes (printed pp.134–137). The Jordan adaptation dropped this festival from the Pupil's Book (which keeps F1 Months and seasons and F3 Independence Day, preserving the F3 numbering) but the Teacher's Book was never updated to remove it. A teacher following this guide would prepare a unit the pupils' books do not contain.
- Edition mismatch: guide is first edition 2024 (for 2024/2025); the pupil books in hand are the 2025 revised second edition.
- Tests (unit and end-of-semester) are referenced as digital resources, not printed here.

# Jordan — English Grade 3 Jordan Team Together, Grade 3: Pupil's Book, Semester 2 — FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-03 (anchors 20/20)

## The book itself
- Verified title (cover + imprint p.ii): *Jordan Team Together, Grade 3: Pupil's Book, Semester 2* — "Pupil's Book with Digital Resources". Series: Jordan Team Together (Pearson Education Limited and York Press Ltd), National Center for Curriculum Development, Amman.
- Author: Lesley Koustaff, with Kay Bentley and Sarah McConnell (thanked). Evaluation and Adaptation Committee: Prof. Nayel Darweesh Al-Shara'h (Head), Dr. Manal Fahed Aburumman (Coordinator), Prof. Abdallah Ahmad Bani Abdelrahman, Prof. Sabri Shehadeh AlShboul, Dr. Zeina Mohammad AlKaraki, Dr. Adnan Ahmad Al-Omari.
- Edition: **First edition (trial) 2024** — this copy. ISBN 978-9923-41-734-8. National Library deposit (2024/10/5998). Board of Education decision No. 149/2024; adopted for the 2024/2025 academic year.
- Copy facts: PDF 76 pages; 48,506,092 bytes. Printed folio = PDF page − 2 (printed p.4 = PDF p.6); content printed pp.4–49 (PDF 6–51), credits PDF p.52, cut-outs PDF pp.53–76.
- Text-layer state: full text layer on content pages; cut-out appendix is image-only picture cards (visually spot-checked). A few festival pages show folio misprints in the text layer ("fifty-two" on printed p.44) — the printed page numbers themselves run 44–49 correctly in sequence.

## Full unit/lesson map
Contents: Welcome My favourite day! p.4 · 5 Fun on the farm p.8 · 6 Out in the forest p.16 · 7 Look at the stars p.24 · 8 Animals are fun! p.32 · Language booster 2 p.40 · Social Science: What can we see on a map? p.42 · Festivals: National Olive Festival p.44, Eid al-Fitr p.46, Aqaba Arts Festival p.48 · Cutouts p.51.

- **Welcome — My favourite day!** (pp.4–7): revision of Semester 1 (activities, clothes, jobs; present simple I/We and He/She; Do you…? / Does he/she…?). Atomic's favourite day is Friday — he doesn't get up on Fridays. Project: poster of your favourite day.
- **Unit 5 — Fun on the farm** (pp.8–15): Farm animals: chicken, cow, donkey, duck, goat, horse, sheep, bird, bee, turkey. Farm activities: brush the horses, clean the cage, collect the eggs, fix the gate, milk the cows, sweep the barn. Grammar: present continuous questions — Is he/she/it (flying)? Yes, he/she/it is. / No, he/she/it isn't. Are they (brushing the horses)? Yes, they are. / No, they aren't. Plurals: one horse – two horses; one sheep – two sheep. Story: helping Uncle Bob on the farm; the goats run after Polly (Values: Pay attention; Look after animals). Culture/Project: "A day on the farm" — farm visits and picnics in Jordan, collecting olives and eggs; make a farm collage. English in action: Asking for clarification (Sorry, can you repeat that?). Phonics: ng, -ing (king, ring; milking, singing).
- **Unit 6 — Out in the forest** (pp.16–23): Adjectives: dirty, clean, old, new, plain, fancy, hard, soft, wet, dry. Camping objects: tent, sleeping bag, shampoo, toothpaste, sunscreen, torch. Grammar: have got — I haven't got (a new book); He/She hasn't got (a soft ball); Have you got (a tent)? Yes, I have. / No, I haven't. Has he/she got…? Yes, he/she has. / No, he/she hasn't. Story: "The map problem" — a wet map, Amal's compass, and finding the cabin (Values: Be resourceful; Don't leave your things in nature). Culture/Project: cooking **Zarb** (traditional Jordanian barbecue cooked in a barrel in the ground) and **sajiyeh**; make a recipe book of popular camping food. English in action: Talking about possession (Whose torch is this? It's mine. / It's his/hers.). Phonics: cl, fl, sl (clean, cliff; flag, flip flop; sleep, slug).
- **Unit 7 — Look at the stars** (pp.24–31): Furniture: door, window, sofa, armchair, television, bed, wardrobe, mirror, clock, bookcase. Prepositions of place: in front of, between, opposite, behind, next to (extending in/on/under). Grammar: There's a (bookcase) / There are (bookcases); There isn't a (chair) / There aren't any (beds). Story: a clue hunt at Tom's house ending in a telescope surprise from Mum and Dad — looking at the stars and the Moon (Value: Work together). Culture/Project: "Log cabins" — log cabins in the USA and in Ajloun, Jordan (fireplace, log walls and ceilings); make a dream-house poster. English in action: Talking about location (Is it behind the sofa?). Phonics: ck (clock, black, backpack, truck).
- **Unit 8 — Animals are fun!** (pp.32–39): Wild animals: monkey, tiger, hippo, giraffe, zebra, elephant, crocodile, penguin, eagle, kangaroo. Food: coconut, burger, kiwi, grapes, mango, ice lolly. Grammar: ability — Can (penguins) swim? Yes, they can. / Can (hippos) jump? No, they can't; existence — Is there a (coconut)? Yes, there is. / No, there isn't. Are there any (burgers)? Yes, there are. / No, there aren't. Story: "A play at the zoo" — animal facts in costume (penguins can swim, can't fly, eat fish; elephants can't jump; monkeys climb trees) (Values: Eat healthy food; Respect animals). Culture/Project: "Helping wild animals" — animal rescue centres in Jordan; the Sinai rosefinch, the national bird of Jordan; brown bears at a rescue centre; make a brochure about an animal rescue centre. English in action: Talking about feelings (I'm thirsty. You should have some orange juice. Would you like an apple?). Phonics: cr, fr, gr (crab; frog; green).
- **Language booster 2** (pp.40–41): places in town — park, bakery, department store, post office, supermarket, bank, chemist's, bookshop — with past simple was/wasn't (I was at the department store yesterday. I wasn't at the park.) and asking/giving directions (Excuse me. Where's the bakery? It's on Green Street. It's next to the bookshop.).
- **Social Science (CLIL)** (pp.42–43): "What can we see on a map?" — maps vs satellite images; compass points north, south, east, west; make a map of a town (bus station, hospital, fountain, museum).
- **Festivals** (pp.44–49): National Olive Festival (olives, oil, taste, demonstrations, pour, bottle, dip, soap; farmers pick olives starting in September; make an olive-oil and watercolour painting); Eid al-Fitr (meat, Arabic coffee, sweets, money, feast, charity, crescent moon, lantern; make a lantern); Aqaba Arts Festival (stall, bowl, bracelet, basket, necklace, henna painting, rug, workshop; design a rug).

## Grammar and vocabulary spine for the book
- Present continuous questions (Is he…ing? / Are they…ing?) for actions now.
- Have got / haven't got / Has…got? for possession, with describing adjectives.
- There is/are (+ any) with furniture and extended prepositions (in front of, between, opposite, behind, next to).
- Can/can't for animal abilities; Is there/Are there any…? for food.
- First past-tense forms appear only in the Language booster: was/wasn't for places in town yesterday.
- Phonics: ng/-ing → cl/fl/sl → ck → cr/fr/gr.

## Honest negatives
- First (trial) edition only; no revised edition in the set.
- The Language booster's was/wasn't is the book's only past simple — it is presented as a booster, not a main unit, and there is no was/were plural teaching.
- Cut-out pages are picture-only; the festival folio words in the text layer are misprinted on two pages ("fifty-two"/"fifty-three" on pp.44–45) though the printed numerals are sequential.

- Verified title (cover + imprint p.ii): *Jordan Team Together, Grade 3: Activity Book, Semester 2* — "Activity Book with Digital Resources", workbook companion to the Grade 3 Pupil's Book Semester 2 (Jordan Team Together, Pearson/York Press, National Center for Curriculum Development, Amman). Printed folio = PDF page − 2 (contents printed p.1 = PDF p.3; picture dictionary printed pp.43–48 = PDF pp.45–50; PDF p.51 blank; credits PDF p.52). Full text layer except the blank page.

## Full unit/lesson map
Contents (printed): Welcome My favourite day! p.2 · 5 Fun on the farm p.6 · 6 Out in the forest p.14 · 7 Look at the stars p.22 · 8 Animals are fun! p.30 · Language booster 2 p.38 · Festivals: National Olive Festival p.40, Eid al-Fitr p.41, Aqaba Arts Festival p.42 · Picture dictionary p.43.
Same per-unit pattern as Semester 1's Activity Book (vocabulary/grammar, story Lesson 4, skills Lesson 7, culture Lesson 8, English in action + phonics Lessons 9–10, Extra practice).

- **Welcome** (pp.2–5): story ticking (what the children do); routines writing; present-simple grammar (I play / He skateboards; Does he/she…? Yes, he does. / No, she doesn't.); jobs and clothes circling.
- **Unit 5 Farm** (pp.6–13): PB p.8 questions (they go to the farm by…; motorbike colour; cats); farm animals follow-and-write; Is it running/flying/walking/climbing? Yes, it is. / No, it isn't.; story true/false (Polly loves cows; the goats run after Laila — corrected in the key sense to the PB story); Are they…? farm-activity questions; skills: read "A farm visit" about Noura's family helping on her uncle's farm and answer Is/Are questions; culture circle-the-answer (Jordan farms have olive trees; you can collect the olives); phonics ng/-ing writing (ring, sweep-ing, sing, jump, wing, milk); "Asking for clarification" dialogue; Extra practice present-continuous writing.
- **Unit 6 Forest** (pp.14–21): PB p.16 questions; adjectives match-and-write (plain, dirty, dry, soft, old ↔ new, clean, hard, fancy, wet); have got/hasn't got follow-and-circle; story correction ("The map problem" — Bo's got a yoyo, not a purple kite; Tom's sweater is wet; they walk to a cabin); camping objects unscramble (torch, toothpaste, sunscreen, shampoo, sleeping bag, tent) and Has she got…? questions; skills: read "A camping weekend" (Jamal's family — and they haven't got a tent!) and write about your own camping things; culture: order the Zarb cooking steps; phonics cl/fl/sl (cliff, flip flop, sleep, clean, slug, flag); "Talking about possession" dialogue; Extra practice crossword (shampoo, sleeping bag, torch, toothpaste, tent, sunscreen).
- **Unit 7 Stars** (pp.22–29): PB p.24 questions (who is in the bedroom; armchairs; are the pyjamas dirty); furniture write-and-match; There's a/There are + singular/plural circling (bed/beds, mirror/mirrors…); story ordering ("Fun at Tom's house" clue texts); prepositions in front of/between/opposite/behind/next to with There isn't a/There aren't any…; skills: read "In the attic" (Omar's attic — an armchair behind the bookcase, toys on the bookcase) and write about a room in your home; culture Yes/No on log cabins (incl. the false statement "There aren't any log cabins in Ajloun"); phonics ck/ch/sh/th sorting and the "Talking about location" dialogue (I can't find my phone… Is it between the bookcases?).
- **Unit 8 Animals** (pp.30–37): PB p.32 questions; wild-animal read-and-circle; Can kangaroos fly? No, they can't. — ability questions for monkeys, zebras, hippos, crocodiles, giraffes, penguins, elephants; story: identify who wears each animal costume/mask (crocodile, tiger, giraffe, monkey, elephant, penguin); food wordsearch (kiwi, coconut, mango, burger, ice lolly, grapes) and Is there a…? / Are there any…? circling; skills: read "At the zoo" (no eagles or penguins; giraffes and tigers can run and jump; elephants and hippos can run but can't jump) and write your own zoo text; culture read-and-write on rescue centres (Are there rescue centres in Jordan? Yes, there are. Can people feed the animals? No, they can't.); phonics fr/gr/cr (grapes, Friday, grey, crocodile, fruit); "Talking about feelings" lesson.
- **Language booster 2** (pp.38–39): places in town with was/wasn't (Maha text: write was or wasn't for supermarket, bakery, post office, park, bookshop); read-and-order the directions dialogue (the bakery is on Green Street, next to the bookshop); read-and-write where Hanan, Heba, Amal and Malak were yesterday.
- **Festivals** (pp.40–42): National Olive Festival (complete the text: farmers talk about their olives, watch cookery demonstrations, taste olive oil, buy bottles and soap); Eid al-Fitr (look-read-number: feast, money, meat, crescent moon, Arabic coffee, sweets; complete the text — Eid begins with a crescent moon in the sky; families have feasts, drink Arabic coffee, give money to charity, decorate houses with lanterns); Aqaba Arts Festival (stalls, necklaces, bowls, rugs, henna painting, workshops).
- **Picture dictionary** (pp.43–48): W Clothes and jobs; 5 Farm animals and activities; 6 Adjectives and camping objects; 7 Furniture and prepositions of place; 8 Wild animals and food; F Festivals word bank.

## Grammar and vocabulary spine for the book
- Written consolidation of the Pupil's Book Semester 2 spine: present-continuous questions; have/has got and negatives; There is/are with prepositions; can/can't; Is there/Are there any…?; booster-level was/wasn't.
- Personal writing frames in Skills pages (my farm visit, my camping things) parallel the Pupil's Book projects.
- Phonics mirrors the Pupil's Book: ng/-ing, cl/fl/sl, ck, cr/fr/gr.

## Honest negatives
- No answer key in the Activity Book (keys in the Teacher's Book).
- This file is far more heavily compressed than its companions (7.3 MB vs ~31 MB for Semester 1's Activity Book); the cover render is greyscale. Text and page count are complete — the difference is image compression, not missing content.
- In the Unit 5 story true/false exercise, one statement says the goats are running after Laila, whereas the Pupil's Book story has the goats running after Polly — as a true/false item this reads as a deliberate false statement, but the name swap is recorded here as found.

# Jordan — English Grade 4 Jordan Team Together, Grade 4: Pupil's Book, Semester 1 — FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-03 (anchors 18/18)

## The book itself
- Series: **Jordan Team Together** (Jordanian adaptation of Pearson's *Team Together*), published for the Hashemite Kingdom of Jordan by the National Center for Curriculum Development and Evaluation (NCCD), Amman; original © Pearson Education Limited and York Press Ltd.
- Verified title (imprint indexing data): *Jordan Team Together, Grade 4: Pupil's Book, Semester 1*. Cover banner reads "Pupil's Book with Digital Resources" and shows Petra.
- **Identity surprise:** the cover and contents-page artwork banners in this file read "Semester 2", but the imprint/indexing page, the contents themselves (Welcome + Units 1–4) and the embedded text layer all say **Semester 1**. The book is the Semester 1 book; the front-matter banner artwork was evidently reused from Semester 2.
- Edition: First edition (trial) 2025; **Second edition (revised and updated) 2026** — this file. Adopted by Ministry of Education decision No. 33/2025 for the 2026/2027 academic year. Printed 2025, reprinted 2026.
- ISBN 978-9923-66-051-5; National Library deposit (2026/1/391); classification 375.001.
- Author: Michelle Mahony, with Kay Bentley and Tessa Lochowski (thanked for their contribution).
- Adaptation Committee in Jordan: Prof. Fawwaz Mohammad Al-Abed Al-Haq (Head); Dr. Manal Fahed Aburumman (Coordinator); Prof. Sabri Shehadeh AlShboul; Dr. Zeina Mohammad AlKaraki; Dr. Adnan Ahmad Al-Omari.
- File: `grade-04_gov_jordan_english-language_student_term-1.pdf`, 41,345,920 bytes, 60 PDF pages.
- Page offset: front matter is roman-numbered (cover = i, imprint = ii, contents = iii), then a 2-page Scope and sequence; printed page = PDF page − 2 (printed p.4 = PDF p.6; printed p.57 = PDF p.59). Acknowledgements on PDF p.60 (printed 58).
- Cast: Hamed, a Jordanian boy who has moved from Aqaba to Amman; British sisters Katy and Millie next door; Bo the robot; the "Discovery Team" framing.
- Structure of every unit (10 printed pages): opener picture page → Lesson 1 Vocabulary → Story (comic) → Lesson 2 story follow-up → Grammar lessons → Lesson 5 Vocabulary and Grammar → Lesson 7 reading/culture → Lesson 8 English in action (song/chant) → Lesson 9 Skills → Lesson 10 writing. A Scope and sequence at the front gives objectives, vocabulary, grammar and all four skills per unit.

## Full unit/lesson map
- **Welcome — New neighbours** (printed 4–7). Vocabulary: countries (Egypt, Jordan, Qatar, Spain, the United Kingdom, the United Arab Emirates); nationalities (British, Egyptian, Emirati, Jordanian, Qatari, Spanish); hobbies (canoeing, going shopping, playing basketball/football/table tennis/tennis, reading, swimming); numbers 1–100. Grammar: revision of *to be* and *like* ("I like / He likes swimming"; "Where are you from? I'm from Jordan. I'm Jordanian."). Story: Hamed's family moves in next door to Katy and Millie in Amman. Project: writing and presenting riddles.
- **Unit 1 — New school** (8–17). Vocabulary: school subjects (Arabic, Art, Digital Skills, English, Islamic Studies, Maths, PE, Science, Social Studies); people/places in school (headteacher, school nurse, science lab); adverbs of frequency (always, never, often, sometimes) and phrases (at the weekend, every day, once/twice/three times a week). Grammar: present simple with dates and times ("We have Maths on Tuesdays at 11 o'clock"); present simple with adverbs of frequency ("He plays tennis twice a week"). Story: Hamed's first day at his new school. Lesson 7 reading: schools in the UK. Phonics: -s/-z/-ɪz endings (likes, makes, reads, goes, brushes, watches). Project: poster about your perfect school. Function: making arrangements ("Do you want to play football tomorrow morning?").
- **Unit 2 — Picnic time!** (18–27). Vocabulary: food (cereal, cupcakes, fruit, noodles, olives, salad, sandwiches, vegetables); drink (coffee, lemonade, milkshake, tea); containers (a bag/bottle/bowl/box/can/cup/glass/plate of). Grammar: *there is/there are* with countable and uncountable nouns, *a lot of / a little / any* ("There's a lot of salad and a little lemonade"; "Are there any apples?"). Lesson 7 reading: food around the world. Phonics: /iː/ (key, seat, tea, feet, see; happy, dirty, family). Project: poster about special foods from Jordan. Function: shopping for food ("Can I have six apples, please? … That's five dinars, please.").
- **Unit 3 — Along the river** (28–37). Vocabulary: landscapes (city, forest, hill, island, lake, mountain, river, town, waterfall); adjectives (beautiful, boring, colourful, dangerous, deep, delicious, difficult, exciting, high, interesting, wide). Grammar: comparatives and superlatives, short adjectives ("A mountain is higher than a hill"; "This is the deepest lake in Jordan") and long adjectives ("Swimming is more exciting than walking"; "Amman is the most exciting city in the world"). Lesson 7 reading: national parks around the world. Phonics: /aɪ/ (prize, bike, ride, bedtime, light, goodnight). Project: leaflet about a national park in Jordan. Function: asking the way ("Excuse me, can you tell me the way to the park, please? Turn right at the supermarket…").
- **Unit 4 — All about jobs** (38–47). Vocabulary: jobs (bus driver, carpenter, chef, doctor, farmer, firefighter, mechanic, police officer, scientist, teacher, vet, waiter/waitress); school rules (arrive on time, be late, be quiet in class, break/follow the rules, drop litter, put litter in the bin, shout in class). Grammar: present continuous ("I'm watching Hamed's house"; "Are you wearing a green jacket?"); *must/mustn't* ("You mustn't break the rules at school"; "You must be quiet in class"). Lesson 7 reading: the most unusual jobs in the world (Skills text features a foley artist). Phonics: /eɪ/ (snail, painting, rain, playing, hay, snake, cake). Project: a job book. Function: calling the emergency services ("What service do you need? I need an ambulance / the police / the fire brigade, please.").
- **Bo's Learning Club — Language booster 1** (48–49): consolidation of Units 1–4 language, including prepositions of place (on the bottom shelf, below).
- **Science (CLIL) — Can plants grow in water?** (50–51): water plants and a simple food chain (Sun → plants → animals), group presentation.
- **Festivals** (52–54): World Environment Day (presenter: Mariam), World Friendship Day, Flag Day — one page each with reading and a make/share activity.
- **Solve it** (55): problem-solving page recapping the unit stories (Units 1–4, Pupil's Book pp. 10–11, 20–21, 30–31, 40–41).
- **Grammar reference** (56) and **Irregular verbs** list (57: be–was/were … see–saw, wear–wore). Acknowledgements (58).

## Grammar and vocabulary spine for the book
Grammar sequence: *to be* + *like* revision and introductions (Welcome) → present simple for timetables, dates/times and frequency (U1) → *there is/are*, countability and quantity (U2) → comparatives/superlatives (U3) → present continuous + *must/mustn't* (U4). Vocabulary is thematic (countries/nationalities, school, food and containers, landscapes and adjectives, jobs and rules) with a phonics strand (-s endings, /iː/, /aɪ/, /eɪ/), a Jordan-linked project per unit, and functional dialogues (arrangements, shopping, directions, emergency call). Currency and setting are Jordanian (dinars, Aqaba, Amman, Wadi Rum, Ajloun Castle).

## Honest negatives
- No separate answer key or audioscripts in the Pupil's Book (they live in the Teacher's Book); listening tracks are referenced by codes (W1, W2…) only.
- The cover/contents banner mislabels this file "Semester 2" (see identity note above) — the semester must be taken from the imprint and contents, not the banner.
- No irregular-verb past participles are given (infinitive + past simple only).
- Text-layer extraction of the cover emblem is garbled (circular ministry seal); identity rests on the rendered pages, which are clear.
- Citation check: 18/18 anchors verified against the PDF page-by-page text (e.g. "from Aqaba" PDF p.6; "Katy and Millie are sisters" PDF p.7; "Schools in the UK" PDF p.16; "There are a lot of noodles" PDF p.24; "food chain" PDF p.53; "break the rules at school" PDF p.58).

- **Festivals practice** (40–42): World Environment Day ("Look at Pupil's Book page 52 and complete the sentences"), World Friendship Day (puzzle with a secret word), Flag Day (unscramble letters and match).
- **Communication activities** (43–47): Pupil A / Pupil B pair pages for Units 1 and 3, and the Unit 2 shopping dialogue ("Can I help you?").

## Grammar and vocabulary spine for the book
Mirrors the Pupil's Book exactly: introductions and numbers (Welcome) → present simple/frequency (U1) → there is/are and quantity (U2) → comparatives/superlatives (U3) → present continuous and must/mustn't (U4), practised in writing, sorting, matching, puzzles and short guided writing, with the Wordlist as the reference spine.

- Edition: **First edition (trial) 2025** — this file. Adopted by Board of Education decision No. 210/2025 for the 2025/2026 academic year.
- ISBN 978-9923-863-26-8; National Library deposit (2025/10/6126); classification 375.001.
- Author: Michelle Mahony, with Kay Bentley and Tessa Lochowski.
- Evaluation and Adaptation Committee: Prof. Fawwaz Mohammad Al-Abed Al-Haq (Head); Dr. Manal Fahed Aburumman (Coordinator); Prof. Sabri Shehadeh AlShboul; Dr. Zeina Mohammad AlKaraki; Dr. Adnan Ahmad Al-Omari.

- Page offset: printed page = PDF page − 2 (printed p.4 = PDF p.6; printed p.57 = PDF p.59); front matter roman-numbered i–iii plus a 2-page Scope and sequence; acknowledgements PDF p.60.
- **File condition:** this copy carries a diagonal red Arabic trial-copy watermark across the pages (also visible in the text layer as stray Arabic glyphs). Text remains fully legible and extractable.
- Same cast (Hamed, Katy, Millie, Bo the robot, Discovery Team) and same 10-page unit architecture as Semester 1.

## Full unit/lesson map
- **Welcome — Hello from Hamed!** (printed 4–7). A revision unit: school subjects and people in a school, food and drink, jobs, landscapes — revising Semester 1 vocabulary and grammar through a letter from Hamed, who is visiting family in Ajloun (he paints the forests of Ajloun).
- **Unit 5 — Hobbies** (8–17). Vocabulary: freetime activities (build a robot, dress up, go fishing, go sailing, go windsurfing, kick, knit, play board games, play chess, play computer games, sew, skip); adverbs of manner (badly, carefully, easily, loudly, quickly, quietly, slowly, well). Grammar: *can/can't* ("I can go sailing"; "Can you sew? Yes, I can. / No, I can't"); adverbs of manner ("She can sew well"). Lesson 7: ball games and their rules (poster: play on one side of the net). Phonics: /əʊ/ (robot, boat, coat, hello, nose, rose, open). Project: rules poster for a ball game; designing a map for an activity day. Function: talking about preferences ("What shall we do? … Not really. I'd rather play chess.").
- **Unit 6 — Our town** (18–27). Vocabulary: places in the local area (bridge, bus stop, car park, clothes shop, hotel, market, museum, restaurant, shopping centre, square, train station, zoo); adjectives (ancient, busy, cheap, expensive, modern, noisy, quiet, safe). Grammar: past simple of *to be* ("She was at the zoo last week"; "Where were you yesterday morning?"); *there was/there were* ("There was an expensive shop"; "Was there a restaurant? Yes, there was. / No, there wasn't"). Story: the children discover their town had a river next to the square 150 years ago. Lesson 7: historic buildings and a timeline poster. Phonics: /ɔɪ/ (boy, coin, oil, point, soil, toy, boil). Function: retelling the story; making recommendations ("It was great. You should see it!").
- **Unit 7 — Our history** (28–37). Vocabulary: historical finds and materials (clay, coins, helmet, iron, jewellery, jug, mosaic, necklace, ring, shield, stone, wall); verbs (arrive, live, need, start, stop, use, visit, work). Grammar: past simple, regular verbs ("People in the past used clay to build things"; "They didn't use plastic"; "Did you visit the museum? Yes, I did. / No, I didn't"). Story: a find at the museum with Mr Omar. Lesson 7 reading: The History of Jordan (people have lived in Jordan for thousands of years; work with towers on a large map). Skills text: a Roman boy, Marcus, watching shows in the Colosseum. Phonics: past-tense endings /d/, /t/, /ɪd/ (arrived, worked, stopped, visited, lived, needed). Project: map of historic places; making a coin. Function: giving advice ("You should work carefully").
- **Unit 8 — Let's celebrate!** (38–47). Vocabulary: celebrations (big wheel, bride, candle, costume, family gathering, funfair, groom, invitation, present, games, rollercoaster, wedding); ordinal numbers first–thirty-first. Grammar: past simple, irregular verbs ("I wore this dress at our wedding"; "We didn't see the ring again"; "Did you get any presents?"). Story strand includes a trip to Petra. Lesson 7: culture — festivals around the world. Writing: an invitation, with times (10 am = in the morning) and "RSVP". Phonics: /aʊ/ (brown, town, cloud, cow, loud, mouth, count). Project: festival leaflet; designing a ring (class vote: most beautiful, most original). Function: explaining you've lost something ("I can't find my bag.").
- **Bo's Learning Club — Language booster 2** (48–49): consolidation of Units 5–8, including talking about what you did when you got home from school yesterday.
- **Social Studies (CLIL) — What was transport like 100 years ago?** (50–51): old vehicles; "One hundred years ago, trains were loud and dirty"; group presentations of old vehicles.
- **Festivals** (52–54): World Wildlife Day (including the desert hedgehog; poster display), Eid al-Adha (giving to family, friends, teacher, community), International Literacy Day (recommending a book to a friend).
- **Solve it** (55); **Grammar reference** (56: Units 5–8, e.g. "Did she wear a costume? Yes, she did. / No, she didn't"); **Irregular verbs** (57). Acknowledgements PDF p.60.

## Grammar and vocabulary spine for the book
The semester is the past-tense semester: *can/can't* + adverbs of manner (U5) → past simple of *to be* and *there was/were* (U6) → past simple regular verbs (U7) → past simple irregular verbs + ordinals (U8). Vocabulary themes move outward from the child (hobbies) to town, history and celebrations, with Jordanian content (Ajloun, Petra, the History of Jordan lesson) and a phonics strand /əʊ/, /ɔɪ/, -ed endings, /aʊ/.

## Honest negatives
- This file is watermarked as a trial copy (red Arabic overlay on every page); it is not a clean print master.
- No answer key or audioscripts in the Pupil's Book itself.
- The Welcome unit is revision only — no new grammar is introduced there.
- Citation check: 18/18 anchors verified against the PDF (e.g. "Hello from Hamed" PDF p.6; "forests of Ajloun" PDF p.9; "150 years ago" PDF p.22; "THE HISTORY OF JORDAN" PDF p.36; "went to Petra" PDF p.42; "Did she wear a costume" PDF p.58).

- Each unit follows the Semester 1 rhythm: Lesson 1 Vocabulary notes + facsimile, Story notes with the "How to work with stories" routine, Grammar, Lesson 5 Vocabulary and Grammar, Lesson 7 Culture, Lesson 8 English in action, Lessons 9–10 Skills.

## Grammar and vocabulary spine for the book
Same spine as the Pupil's Book Semester 2 (can/can't + manner adverbs → was/were, there was/were → past simple regular → past simple irregular + ordinals), wrapped in the series' GSE/AfL/differentiation method.

## Honest negatives
- Trial-copy watermark throughout this file.
- No separate audioscript/answer-key appendix and no printed test papers — scripts and keys are inside the lesson notes; assessment support is formative (rubrics and self-assessment cards).
- At 132 PDF pages this guide is two pages shorter than the Semester 1 guide (one Notes spread fewer; acknowledgements replace the final image-credits page).

## The book itself
- Series: **Jordan Team Together**, National Center for Curriculum Development and Evaluation (NCCD), Amman; © Pearson Education Limited and York Press Ltd. Cover: "Pupil's Book with Digital Resources", Grade 5, Semester 1, with the Jerash columns as the cover image.
- Verified title (imprint indexing data): *Jordan Team Together, Grade 5: Pupil's Book, Semester 1*.
- Edition: First edition (trial) 2024; **Second edition (revised and updated) 2025** — this file. Adopted by Board of Education decision No. 44/2024 for the 2025/2026 academic year. Printed 2024, reprinted 2025–2026.
- ISBN 978-9923-41-914-4; National Library deposit (2025/3/1595); classification 375.001.
- Author: Michelle Mahony, with Kay Bentley and Tessa Lochowski (publishers also thank Gareth Vaughan).
- Adaptation Committee in Jordan: Prof. Nayel Darweesh Al-Shara'h (Head); Dr. Manal Fahed Aburumman (Coordinator); Dr. Hasan Hasan El-Kanash; Saad Mohammad Odeh. (Different head from the Grade 4 committee.)
- File: `grade-05_gov_jordan_english-language_student_term-1.pdf`, 84,757,760 bytes, 64 PDF pages.
- Page offset: printed page = PDF page − 2 (printed p.4 = PDF p.6; Grammar reference printed 59 = PDF p.61). Two blank PDF pages (62–63) precede the acknowledgements page (PDF p.64).
- Cast continues: Hamed, Katy, Millie and Bo the robot (the "Discovery Team"). Grade 5 units are longer: **12 lessons per unit** (Lessons 1–2 opener/vocabulary, story at Lessons 3–4, Grammar at Lesson 5–6, Vocabulary and Grammar at Lesson 7–8, Culture at Lesson 9, English in action at Lesson 10, Skills at Lesson 11, writing at Lesson 12), and Lesson 9 pages carry an "Ask Siraj!" feature.

## Full unit/lesson map
- **Welcome — Welcome back** (printed 4–7). Vocabulary: free-time activities (play football, play basketball, go swimming, Science Club, visit Granny, tennis lesson); appearance (curly/long/straight/short/dark/fair hair, a beard, a moustache). Grammar: present simple with *before/after/when* ("When Jameela gets home, she plays with her cat"). Project: a poem about your family, presented to the class.
- **Unit 1 — Talent show** (8–17). Vocabulary: personality adjectives (shy, confident, lazy, hard-working, friendly, cheerful, kind, quiet, polite, rude, tidy, untidy); hobbies (count, spell, do experiments, bake a cake, throw a ball, fix toys, juggle, make a pizza). Grammar: comparative and superlative adjectives ("My mum is shorter than me"; "She's the shortest person in our family!"); *be good at* ("I'm good at baking a cake"). Pronunciation/word-building: the prefix *un-* (untidy, unfriendly, unhappy, unlucky, unwell, unsafe). Lesson 9 Culture: good manners around the world. Project: a good manners book. Function: describing people ("She's very friendly").
- **Unit 2 — Then and now** (18–27). Vocabulary: technology (computer, email, smartwatch, keyboard, letter, mouse, telephone, tablet, mobile phone); verb phrases (post a letter, hold a pen, send an email, win a race, make a face, make a sandwich, tell a story, tie a shoelace). Grammar: past simple ("Did you have a phone ten years ago? That computer was really big!"); *could/couldn't* ("When I was one, I could walk but I couldn't talk"). Word-building: suffix *-tion* (inform → information, describe → description). Story: the team invents a secret "Team code". Project: a guide to a museum. Function: asking for information ("I'd like some information about the Science Museum, please").
- **Bo's Learning Club — Language booster 1** (28–29): dialogues suggesting activities ("That's a great idea! I love baking. … Let's bake a cake then!").
- **Social Studies (CLIL) — Are all the planets rocky?** (30–31): planets and the Moon, fact files.
- **Unit 3 — Let's explore!** (32–41). Vocabulary: space (Sun, star, rocket, Moon, solar system, space station, Earth, telescope, spacesuit, planet, comet, satellite); big numbers (a hundred, five hundred, a thousand, five thousand, ten thousand, fifty thousand, a hundred thousand, a million). Grammar: *will/won't* for future plans ("I'll get him a present. I won't get him a book"); *How…?* questions ("How deep is the Grand Canyon?"). Pronunciation: syllable stress in two- and three-syllable words (planet, spacesuit, rocket, satellite, astronaut, telescope, scientist, explorer). Lesson 9 reading: "The Mystery of…" — stone circles (standing stones). Project: a class book about ancient places; designing a bed for an astronaut. Function: giving personal information (joining the Space Explorers Club).
- **Unit 4 — Off to the shops** (42–51). Vocabulary: shops (butcher's, newsagent's, phone shop, greengrocer's, jeweller's, corner shop, fishmonger's, sports shop, shoe shop, pharmacy, gift shop, toy shop); money and prices (money, save, purse, spend, wallet, buy, birthday money, sell). Grammar: relative clauses ("It's the man who we saw earlier"; "We're in the shop where my brother works"); *have to / don't have to* ("I have to save some money"; "Does he have to buy that boat?"). Word-building: suffix *-ment* (pay → payment, encourage → encouragement). Story: a phone theft at the shops is foiled. Writing focus: the possessive shop names ("the greengrocer's shop = shop of the greengrocer"). Project: a TV advert for a corner shop; a phone case design. Function: asking for a price ("How much is it? It's fifteen dinars and fifty piastres").
- **Bo's Learning Club — Language booster 2** (52–53): animals and what they can do (hop, turtle, climb), healthy food groups and a healthy plate.
- **Science (CLIL) — How can plants keep us healthy?** (54–55): food groups, vitamins and minerals; comparing healthy plates.
- **Festivals** (56–57): Harvest Festival (presenter: Diya), World Water Day (presenter: Farid).
- **Solve it** (58); **Grammar reference** (59: Units 1–4, including relative clauses for person/thing/place and have to). Acknowledgements on the final page (PDF p.64). Note: unlike Grade 4, there is **no separate Irregular verbs page** in the contents — the list lives in the Activity Book/Teacher's Book.

## Grammar and vocabulary spine for the book
Welcome (present simple with before/after/when; appearance) → comparatives/superlatives + be good at (U1) → past simple + could/couldn't (U2) → will/won't + big numbers (U3) → relative clauses + have to (U4). A word-building strand runs through the grade (prefix un-, suffixes -tion and -ment), and every unit ends in a project and a functional dialogue. Jordanian money (dinars and piastres) is used in the shops unit.

## Honest negatives
- Two blank pages sit inside the PDF before the acknowledgements (PDF pp.62–63) — the file is complete but padded.
- No separate irregular-verb list page in this Pupil's Book (present in the Grade 4 books and in the Grade 5 Activity Book).
- No answer keys or audioscripts in the Pupil's Book itself.
- Citation check: 18/18 anchors verified against the PDF (e.g. "Welcome back" PDF p.6; "Team code" PDF p.22; "solar system" PDF p.34; "greengrocer" PDF p.44; "Harvest Festival" PDF p.58; "Does he have to buy that boat" in the Grammar reference, PDF p.61).

## The book itself
- Series: **Jordan Team Together**, National Center for Curriculum Development and Evaluation (NCCD), Amman; © Pearson Education Limited and York Press Ltd 2024. Cover: "Pupil's Book with Digital Resources", Grade 5, Semester 2, Jerash columns.
- Verified title (imprint indexing data): *Jordan Team Together, Grade 5: Pupil's Book, Semester 2*.
- Edition: **First edition (trial) 2024** — this file. Adopted by Board of Education decision No. 151/2024 for the 2024/2025 academic year.
- ISBN 978-9923-41-741-6; National Library deposit (2024/10/6009); classification 372.6521.
- Author: Michelle Mahony (publishers thank Kay Bentley, Gareth Vaughan and Tessa Lochowski).
- Evaluation and Adaptation Committee: Prof. Nayel Darweesh Al-Shara'h (Head); Dr. Manal Fahed Aburumman (Coordinator); Prof. Ahmad Hamad Alkhawaldeh; Prof. Majed Abdelkarim Al-Quran; Abeer Jamil Abu Rayyan. (A larger committee than Semester 1's.)
- File: `grade-05_gov_jordan_english-language_student_term-2.pdf`, 58,919,234 bytes, 64 PDF pages.
- Page offset: printed page = PDF page − 2 (printed p.4 = PDF p.6; Grammar reference printed 59 = PDF p.61); image credits PDF p.62, then blank pages to p.64.
- Same cast and 12-lesson unit architecture as Semester 1, including the "Ask Siraj!" Lesson 9 feature.

## Full unit/lesson map
- **Welcome — Welcome back** (printed 4–7). Revision with a past-tense frame: hobbies (bake a cake, do experiments, fix toys, juggle, make a pizza, throw a ball) and technology (call, computer, email, keyboard, letter, mobile phone, smartwatch, study, tablet, telephone). Grammar: past simple ("I learnt how to cook"; "What did you do, Katy?"). In the story the Discovery Team returns from the holidays — Katy did experiments at Science Club, Millie learnt to bake a cake and fixed toys, Bo juggles five balls. Function: presenting a hobby to the class.
- **Unit 5 — Animals in danger** (8–17). Vocabulary: sea animals (dolphin, jellyfish, lobster, octopus, oyster, ray, seahorse, seal, shark, squid, turtle, whale); land animals (camel, gorilla, koala, lion, panda, parrot, polar bear, rhino). Grammar: past continuous ("The shark was looking for small sea animals"); past continuous vs past simple ("I was watching the koalas when the lion escaped"). Word-building: prefix *dis-* (appear/disappear, like/dislike, agree/disagree, connect/disconnect). Story opens at Aqaba aquarium (jellyfish, turtle, seahorse). Lesson 9 reading: "Helping animals" — animal protection in Jordan, with a timeline back to 2000. Project: a spider diagram about an animal protection organisation. Function: reminding someone to do something ("Remember to change the cat's food and water every day").
- **Unit 6 — Staying healthy** (18–27). Vocabulary: illnesses (a cold, a cough, a headache, a high temperature, a rash, a runny nose, a sore throat, a stomach ache, a toothache, an earache, dizzy, sick); healthy lifestyles (do exercise, eat a balanced diet, eat junk food, fit, healthy, relax, unfit, unhealthy). Grammar: *should/shouldn't* ("You should go to the doctor's! You shouldn't go to school"); infinitive of purpose ("He went to the supermarket to buy fruit"). Word-building: prefixes *un-* and *ir-* (balanced/unbalanced, healthy/unhealthy, fit/unfit, regular/irregular, resistible/irresistible, responsible/irresponsible). Project: design a doctor's waiting room; a healthy lunch. Function: at the doctor's ("What's the matter? I'm feeling sick"; "Have you got a cough?").
- **Bo's Learning Club — Language booster 3** (28–29): naming places (coast etc.) and making definitions.
- **Art (CLIL) — What do landscape paintings look like?** (30–31): landscape/cityscape, foreground/background; describing and making an outdoor scene.
- **Unit 7 — Curtain up!** (32–41). Vocabulary: theatre (act, audience, backstage, cast, cheer, costumes, curtain, director, lighting, props, scenery, stage); entertainment (classical music, comic, concert, country music, festival, musical, poem, puppet show). Grammar: present perfect ("I've read Alice's Adventures in Wonderland. I haven't seen the film"; "He's seen Alice's costume somewhere, but he hasn't found it"). Word-building: suffix *-ful* (cheerful, thankful, playful, fearful, peaceful, forgetful, careful, hopeful). Lesson 9 reading: "Have you ever been to the theatre?" (including a musical, "Roary the Lion"). Writing: playscripts and stage directions in brackets. Project: a poster for a new show. Function: describing clothes ("The trousers are too long. The T-shirt is too small").
- **Unit 8 — Let's get creative!** (42–51). Vocabulary: household appliances (coffee machine, cooker, dishwasher, freezer, fridge, kettle, microwave, oven, sewing machine, toaster, vacuum cleaner, washing machine); materials (cotton, gold, leather, metal, paper, plastic, rubber, silver). Grammar: present perfect with *ever/never* ("Have you ever made chocolates? Yes, I have. / No, I haven't"; "She's never used the oven before"); *is/are made of* ("What are they made of? They're made of gold!"). Pronunciation: stress in two- and three-syllable verbs (begin, contain, decide, enjoy, recommend, understand, entertain, seeing, watching). Lesson 9 reading: "Handmade in Jordan" — traditional crafts; rugs made of sheep's wool. Skills: researching a famous Arab inventor or invention. Project: a class book about traditional Jordanian crafts. Function: explaining that something doesn't work ("Can you help me? You need to turn it on").
- **Bo's Learning Club — Language booster 4** (52–53): countries and continents revision ("Is it Japan? Yes!").
- **Social Studies (CLIL) — How are the continents different?** (54–55): the continents (Asia, Africa, Europe, South America…), poster work.
- **Festivals** (56–57): Youth Service Day (presenter: Carlos, a volunteer), World Heritage Day (presenter: Sana).
- **Solve it** (58); **Grammar reference** (59: Units 5–8 — past continuous, should, infinitive of purpose, present perfect, ever/never, made of). Image credits PDF p.62.

## Grammar and vocabulary spine for the book
Past simple revision (Welcome) → past continuous and past continuous vs past simple (U5) → should/shouldn't + infinitive of purpose (U6) → present perfect (U7) → present perfect with ever/never + passive materials (*made of*) (U8). Word-building continues (dis-, un-/ir-, -ful) and Jordanian content is strong: Aqaba aquarium, animal protection in Jordan, "Handmade in Jordan" crafts, World Heritage Day.

## Honest negatives
- This is the 2024 first (trial) edition; the Semester 1 book in this set is the revised 2025 second edition, so the two semesters here are not the same edition generation.
- No separate irregular-verb list page in the Pupil's Book (it is in the Activity Book and Teacher's Book).
- Blank trailing pages in the PDF (pp.63–64).
- Citation check: 18/18 anchors verified against the PDF (e.g. "juggle five balls" PDF p.6; "Aqaba aquarium" PDF p.11; "HELPING ANIMALS" PDF p.16; "Have you got a cough" PDF p.22; "HAVE YOU EVER BEEN" PDF p.40; "Handmade in Jordan" PDF p.50; "made of gold" in the Grammar reference, PDF p.61).

Team — Arlo, Lara, Alia and Faisal — whose magazine sections (Welcome, Team Talk, Book Club, Culture) structure every unit. Each main unit has 11 numbered lessons (Lessons 1–3, 5–6, 8–11 are the printed pattern; lessons 4 and 7 are the audio/consolidation steps folded into the spreads) plus a unit-opener vocabulary spread.
- Citation check (20/20 anchors verified on the stated printed pages of this PDF; short fragments only): p.4 "World of Wonder" (magazine premise); p.8 "do archery" (Unit 1 opener); p.10 WOW! Quiz dialogue; p.12 cartoon story, rain stopping athletics; p.14 "Sports and hobbies around the world"; p.15 making/responding to suggestions; p.18 Unit 2 Technology opener; p.20 "What's the matter, Alia?"; p.24 technology helping children study in Jordan; p.28 Language booster 1; p.30 "How can we classify sports?"; p.32 "skyscraper" (Unit 3 opener); p.36 Omar sketching the palace (mystery story); p.38 Petra; p.42 "clear the table" (Unit 4 opener); p.46 Anansi the spider playscript cast; p.48 Rashoof as a traditional Jordanian dish; p.56 Tree Day; p.59 Present perfect in the grammar reference; p.60 writing "a good interview".

## Full unit/lesson map

- **Welcome — The WOW! Team (pp.4–7).** The team introduces itself and the magazine. Vocabulary: personality adjectives — cheerful, confident, friendly, hard-working, kind, lazy, polite, quiet, responsible, shy, tidy, untidy. Grammar: Present simple for identity and appearance (age, hair descriptions). Functions: giving brief reasons for opinions on familiar topics. Lessons: magazine premise and reader page (L1), About Us profiles (L2).
- **Unit 1 — Free-time fun (pp.8–17).** Vocabulary: sports with do/go/play (do archery, athletics, gymnastics; go cycling, ice skating, scooting, skiing, snowboarding; play badminton, hockey, table tennis, volleyball) and hobbies (do photography, puzzles, gardening; draw pictures; go to book fairs/museums; listen to audiobooks; make models; play board games; watch cartoons; write a blog/diary). Grammar: Present simple vs Present continuous; state verbs (prefer, sound, know). Lesson map: L1 vocabulary; L2 Team Talk dialogue (Arlo and Faisal's sports); L3 grammar; L5 Book Club cartoon story (Reem, rain, indoor alternative); L6 vocabulary + state verbs; L8 Culture — sports and hobbies around the world; L9 English in action — making and responding to suggestions; L10 Literacy reading — an interview (model-making hobby); L11 writing an interview. Project: a mini-book about popular free-time activities in Jordan.
- **Unit 2 — Technology (pp.18–27).** Vocabulary: devices (app, device, digital camera, e-reader, headphones, laptop, password, printer, screen, smartphone, speaker, website) and using technology (click on an icon, download an app, go online, press a button, search the internet, send a message, take a selfie, turn on/off, type a password, upload a photo, watch a video). Grammar: could/couldn't and had to/didn't have to (past ability and obligation); comparative adverbs (more quickly than, better). L2 dialogue: Alia cannot send her homework to her teacher. L5 Book Club: a poem about needing information/phones. L8 Culture: technology helping children study in Jordan (free online courses, apps). L9 English in action: asking for help. L10–11 Literacy: reading app/game reviews and writing a review (a drawing app review is the model). Project: a spider diagram about technology and learning.
- **Lara's Learning Club — Language booster 1 (pp.28–29)** and **Think like a scientist! How can we classify sports? (pp.30–31)**: town/city vocabulary and directions booster; a CLIL science spread on classifying sports, with an identification key to practise on (Lesson 4).
- **Unit 3 — Places (pp.32–41).** Vocabulary: places and buildings (apartment building, castle, factory, harbour, hospital, office building, palace, skyscraper, sports centre, stadium, swimming pool, tower) and parts of buildings (ceiling, corner, corridor, entrance, escalator, exit, floor, lift, roof, stairs, steps, wall). Grammar: relative pronouns (who/which/where/when) and the Past continuous. L2 dialogue: getting lost on the way to the stadium, past where Alia lives and where Arlo learnt to swim. L5 Book Club mystery story (Omar drawing the palace). L8 Culture: Petra and ancient cities. L9 English in action: giving directions in a building. L10–11 Literacy: a short story (a bird story in the guide's notes; the writing model is Sultan at the stadium). Project: a tourist brochure about an ancient city.
- **Unit 4 — Happy homes (pp.42–51).** Vocabulary: household chores (clear the table, cook dinner, do the washing-up, dust the furniture, empty the bin, load the dishwasher, put away the clothes, sweep the floor, take out the rubbish, tidy up, vacuum the carpet, water the plants) and cooking (add, bake, boil, chop, cut, fry, mix; fork, knife, spoon, salt, pepper). Grammar: Present perfect with already, just and yet; sense verbs (look, smell, taste, sound, feel). L2 dialogue: tidying up and baking a cake before Dad arrives. L5 Book Club: a playscript — an Anansi the spider folk tale with animal cast. L8 Culture: traditional food around the world, including Jordanian Rashoof. L9 English in action: offering to help. L10–11 Literacy: recipes — reading recipes and writing one (chocolate cookies model). Project: a menu for an international food festival / a new scene for a play.
- **Lara's Learning Club — Language booster 2 (pp.52–53)** and **Think like a scientist! How can we compare materials? (pp.54–55)**: clothes vocabulary booster; CLIL spread comparing materials (properties, Venn diagram practise).
- **Festivals (pp.56–57).** Tree Day (Hanan narrates; Jordan's Tree Day, planting, olives and palm trees) and Belonging to My School Day (Laith narrates a school-community celebration in Zarqa).
- **Reference section (pp.58–62).** Grammar reference (pp.58–59, all four units), How to write… reference (p.60: an interview, a review, a short story, a recipe), English in action reference (p.61: suggestions, asking for help, directions in a building, offering to help), acknowledgements (p.62).

## Grammar and vocabulary spine for the book

Grammar, in teaching order: Present simple (Welcome, identity/appearance) → Present simple vs Present continuous + state verbs (U1) → could/couldn't, had to/didn't have to + comparative adverbs (U2) → relative pronouns + Past continuous (U3) → Present perfect with already/just/yet + sense verbs (U4). Vocabulary fields: personality adjectives; sports and hobbies (collocation-led: do/go/play); technology devices and actions; buildings and their parts; household chores and cooking. Recurring skills spine: every unit runs the same lesson rhythm — vocabulary opener, Team Talk dialogue, grammar, Book Club text genre (cartoon, poem, mystery story, playscript), vocabulary-and-grammar, Culture, English in action (a functional dialogue), Literacy reading, Literacy writing — and the two Learning Club blocks add language boosters plus a "Think like a scientist!" CLIL task (classifying sports; comparing materials).

## Honest negatives

- No answer key, audioscripts or teaching notes in this book — those live in the Teacher's Book; listening tasks here are unusable as text alone (audio referenced by track numbers only, e.g. 1.1, LC1.2).
- The pupil book contains no wordlist of its own — the graded wordlist is in the Activity Book (pp.50–55 there).
- Semester 1 only: Units 5–8 are not here; they are in the Semester 2 Pupil's Book. Nothing in this file teaches the Semester 2 grammar (passive, indefinite pronouns, first conditional, deduction modals).
- The "Second edition (revised and updated)" 2026 imprint means this file is NOT identical to the first-edition 2025 pupil book that the held Teacher's Book (first edition) was written against; small mismatches between the two files are possible and the Teacher's Book notes should be read with that caveat.
- Two PDF pages at the end (PDF pp.63–64 area) are the acknowledgements/image credits and a blank — there is no extra unit hidden after the reference section.

## Full unit/lesson map

- **Welcome — The WOW! Team (pp.2–3).** Lesson 1: team names and magazine completion task. Lesson 2: reading and circling appearance/personality facts about Arlo, Lara and Faisal.
- **Unit 1 — Free-time fun (pp.4–13).** L1 vocabulary (do/go/play collocations); L2 Team Talk completion from Pupil's Book p.10 dialogue; L4 grammar — Present simple and Present continuous, with listening completion; L5 Book Club — who says what in the cartoon story (Pupil's Book p.12); L7 vocabulary and grammar — hobbies completion and state verbs; L8 Culture re-reading (sports and hobbies around the world); L9 English in action — suggestion dialogue completion; L10 Literacy interviews — words in context (freedom, heat, sun cream, energy, eco-lodge, route); L12 writing — contractions, interview questions, write-and-check checklist.
- **Unit 2 — Technology (pp.14–23).** L1 device word completion; L2 dialogue completion (Pupil's Book p.20); L4 grammar — could/couldn't, had to/didn't have to (listening: Lubna and her grandma); L5 Book Club — rhyming words from the poem (Pupil's Book p.22); L7 vocabulary and grammar — technology verbs and comparative adverbs (sentences about Sana); L8 Culture re-reading — technology in Jordan, sentence matching; L9 asking-for-help dialogue completion; L10 Literacy reviews — words in context (solve, play, give, score, program, challenge); L12 writing a review from notes, with checklist.
- **Lara's Learning Club — Language booster 1 (pp.24–25).** Map matching and route-following listening (LC1.3).
- **Unit 3 — Places (pp.26–35).** L1 vocabulary (word puzzle: hospital etc.); L2 dialogue completion (Pupil's Book p.34); L4 grammar — relative pronouns, listening tick task; L5 Book Club — ordering the mystery story events (Pupil's Book p.36); L7 vocabulary and grammar — Past continuous sentence completion; L8 Culture — Petra text, true/false/doesn't-say with explanations; L9 giving-directions dialogue completion (escalator, second floor); L10 Literacy short stories — words in context (sketched, zoomed, yelled, remembered, whispered); L12 writing a short story with checklist.
- **Unit 4 — Happy homes (pp.36–45).** L1 chores vocabulary choice; L2 dialogue completion (Pupil's Book p.44); L4 grammar — Present perfect with already, just and yet; L5 Book Club — who says what in the Anansi playscript (Pupil's Book p.46); L7 vocabulary and grammar — sense verbs look/smell/taste; L8 Culture — traditional food tick task; L9 offering-to-help dialogue completion; L10 Literacy recipes — words in context (unscrambling: tray etc.); L12 writing a recipe (sequencers first/next/then/finally) with checklist.
- **Lara's Learning Club — Language booster 2 (pp.46–47).** Clothes sentence completion; dialogue true/false with explanations (Pupil's Book p.53).
- **Festivals (pp.48–49).** Tree Day picture vocabulary (desert, green space, olives, palm tree, plant, school trip); Belonging to My School Day definitions and picture matching.
- **Wordlist (pp.50–55).** Alphabetical-within-unit graded definitions for Welcome and Units 1–4 plus the Learning Clubs (e.g. book fair, cheerful, confident for Welcome; app, message, online, turn on/off for Unit 2; harbour, shuttlecock, straight on for Unit 3; dust, empty, whisper, yell, zoom for Unit 4).
- **Irregular verbs (pp.56–57).** Three-column table (infinitive / Past simple / Past participle), be–break–…–let/lose/make and onward, with a cover-and-recall instruction.
- **Acknowledgements (p.58).**

## Grammar and vocabulary spine for the book

The Activity Book practises exactly the Pupil's Book spine, in the same order, one spread per point: Present simple/continuous and state verbs (U1) → could/couldn't, had to/didn't have to and comparative adverbs (U2) → relative pronouns and Past continuous (U3) → Present perfect with already/just/yet and sense verbs (U4). Its added value over the pupil book is written production: every unit ends in a Lesson 12 writing task with a self-check checklist (interview, review, short story, recipe), and the back matter (Wordlist pp.50–55, Irregular verbs pp.56–57) is unique to this book.

## Honest negatives

- This is a practice book, not a course book: almost every exercise depends on the Pupil's Book (page and track references throughout) and cannot be taught from alone.
- No answer key is printed in the Activity Book itself; answers are in the Teacher's Book / teacher materials, not here.
- It is titled Activity Book on its cover, contents and imprint — any record calling it "Workbook" is using a generic label; for Grade 6 the book's own word is Activity Book (contrast Grade 8, whose companion really is titled Workbook).
- Semester 1 only: Units 5–8 practice is in the Semester 2 Activity Book. The festivals covered here are only Tree Day and Belonging to My School Day.
- The last PDF page is blank after the acknowledgements; there is no tests section in this book.

## What the guide adds (scripts, keys, method)

- **Answer keys for everything**, embedded in the lesson notes page by page (labelled "Answer key" blocks beside each activity), including the Activity Book answers (notes direct pupils to named Activity Book pages and give/check their answers in class).
- **Audioscripts in situ**: the notes tell the teacher when to play each track, give the answers the listening leads to, and for several tracks print or reference the script; the introduction states the Teacher's Book is where teachers find "all the necessary information such as answer keys and audioscripts". There is no separate audioscript appendix — scripts live inside the lesson notes.
- **Per-lesson method**: every lesson opens with Objectives (lesson aims, target language), a staged Presentation/lead-in, pair and class checking routines, and named differentiation boxes — Diversity Support, Diversity Challenge, Extra activity, Fast finishers, Extension — plus Assessment for Learning prompts.
- **Front-matter method (pp.4–21)**: Introduction to Jordan Team Together (skills, grammar step-by-step approach, culture, mixed-ability support); Course components (Pupil's Book, Activity Book, digital resources, tests — unit tests, two End-of-semester tests and one End-of-year test — and the level posters); a Unit walkthrough spread explaining each lesson type (vocabulary presentation, Book Club literature lessons, Literacy lessons 10–12 with recorded model texts); Assessment (monitoring progress, English Benchmark for ages 6–13, formative techniques such as Happy/Sad face, lollipop sticks, rubrics for CLIL, long-term formative assessment); How to… articles (work with projects, mixed-ability groups, posters); Classroom language list (p.18); Games bank (pp.19–21: word card games, "Salah says", Draw the word, Don't say it!, picture charades).
- **Mapping and objectives pages per unit**: before each unit's notes there is a curriculum Mapping page (Jordan curriculum themes — e.g. Unit 1 Theme 6: Recreation; Unit 2 Theme 3: Science and technology; Units 3–4 Themes 1 Society / 2 Culture) and a Unit objectives page restating the full vocabulary and grammar with the unit aim ("to talk about…").
- **Back matter**: Glossary (p.132, the unit wordlists with IPA transcriptions); Extra resources (pp.133–141): photocopiable Happy/Sad face cards, traffic-light cards, picture charades cards, word charades cards, Don't say it! cards, and the Irregular verbs list.

## Full unit/lesson map

The notes follow the Pupil's Book page for page (pupil printed pp.4–61 = Teacher's Book notes pp.22–131):

- **Welcome (TB pp.22–29; pupil pp.4–7)**: objectives "to talk about personality"; notes for the magazine premise lesson and the About Us Team Talk lesson, with keys for the profile-matching and adjective work.
- **Unit 1 Free-time fun (TB pp.30–49; pupil pp.8–17)**: mapping (Theme 6: Recreation), objectives, then notes for Lessons 1 (vocabulary), 2 (Team Talk), 3 (grammar — Present simple/continuous), 5 (Book Club cartoon), 6 (state verbs), 8 (Culture), 9 (suggestions), 10 (interview reading — key includes the interview's numbered answers), 11 (writing an interview).
- **Unit 2 Technology (TB pp.50–69; pupil pp.18–27)**: mapping (Theme 3: Science and technology); notes for the device vocabulary, the homework-email dialogue, could/had to grammar, the Book Club poem, comparative adverbs, the Jordan technology Culture lesson, asking for help, reviews reading (keys name which reviewer mentions a password, an icon, a selfie…) and review writing.
- **Language booster 1 + Think like a scientist (TB pp.70–77; pupil pp.28–31)**: booster objectives framed as Assessment for Learning; the classifying-sports CLIL lessons with keys for the identification key.
- **Unit 3 Places (TB pp.78–97; pupil pp.32–41)**: mapping (Themes 1–2); relative-pronoun grammar notes with an interview warm-up script in the notes, Past continuous via the mystery story, Petra Culture lesson (tourist brochure project), directions, short-story literacy lessons.
- **Unit 4 Happy homes (TB pp.98–117; pupil pp.42–51)**: mapping; Present perfect already/just/yet notes, Anansi playscript lesson, cooking/sense-verbs lesson, traditional food Culture (menu project), offering help, recipes literacy (the chocolate-cookies model is keyed step by step).
- **Language booster 2 + Think like a scientist (TB pp.118–127; pupil pp.52–55)**: clothes booster; comparing-materials CLIL (the notes name the Venn diagram as the answer to the "what diagram" question).
- **Festivals (TB pp.128–131; pupil pp.56–57)**: Tree Day notes (key facts: Tree Day in Jordan is 15 January; what people plant/do) and Belonging to My School Day notes with differentiation boxes.
- **Reference (TB pp.130–132 in the contents' foliation)**: the pupil Grammar reference reproduced with the notes, then the Glossary.

## Grammar and vocabulary spine for the book

Identical spine to the Pupil's Book (Present simple → Present simple/continuous + state verbs → could/had to + comparative adverbs → relative pronouns + Past continuous → Present perfect already/just/yet + sense verbs), but the Teacher's Book is where the spine is made explicit as teachable steps: each grammar lesson's notes name the target structure, the rule-box answers, the common-error watchpoints and the controlled-to-free practice sequence, and the Scope and sequence (TB pp.2–3) tabulates the whole semester's vocabulary, grammar, skills, pronunciation, projects and functions in one spread.

## Honest negatives

- Edition mismatch in the held set: this Teacher's Book is the first edition (2025, school year 2025/2026, decision 92/2025) while the held Pupil's Book and Activity Book files are the second, revised edition (2026, school year 2026/2027). Page references matched at every anchor checked, but the pairing is cross-edition and a second-edition Teacher's Book may exist.
- The tests it describes (unit tests, end-of-semester and end-of-year tests, English Benchmark) are NOT included in this PDF — they are separate digital/print components.
- Audioscripts are embedded in lesson notes rather than gathered in one appendix, and some listenings are handled by answer key and instruction only; a reader who wants a standalone script booklet will not find one here.
- No Semester 2 content: Units 5–8 notes are in the Semester 2 Teacher's Book.
- The last pages are image credits and blank notes pages — no additional teaching content after the Extra resources card sets.

Adoption: Higher Council meeting No. 8/2025 (16/10/2025), Board of Education decision No. 213/2025 (4/12/2025), for the 2025/2026 academic year. Note the ISBN block differs from the Semester 1 second-edition block (978-9923-66-…) — this is the first-edition Semester 2.

- Citation check (20/20): p.4 "The WOW! Team returns"; p.8 Unit 5 Favourite clothes opener; p.10 sports-kit dialogue (too plain / not colourful enough); p.12 Prince Freddie fairy tale opening; p.14 traditional clothes (Jordanian dress); p.15 shopping for clothes; p.18 Unit 6 Adventures opener; p.20 camping in Wadi Mujib dialogue; p.22 Dana Nature Reserve hiking story; p.24 Scouts culture text; p.28 Language booster 3; p.30 "How can we classify types of energy in diagrams?"; p.32 Unit 7 Entertainment opener; p.36 Aqaba family-holiday diary; p.42 Unit 8 Awesome animals opener; p.46 platypus folk tale; p.48 national animals culture text; p.52 Language booster 4; p.56 Father's Day; p.57 World Oceans Day.

## Full unit/lesson map

- **Welcome — The WOW! Team returns (pp.4–7).** Revision unit: hobbies vocabulary (do photography, do puzzles, do the gardening, draw pictures, make models, write a blog) and technology vocabulary (digital camera, e-reader, headphones, laptop, smartphone, website) recycled from Semester 1; grammar revision — Present perfect with already, just and yet (blog/travel contexts). Lessons built around the team's Favourite Topics page.
- **Unit 5 — Favourite clothes (pp.8–17).** Vocabulary: describing clothes (baggy, casual, checked, colourful, comfortable, formal, patterned, plain, smart, spotted, striped, uncomfortable) and clothes/accessories (belt, bracelet, crown, earrings, glasses, gloves, sweatshirt, tie, top, tracksuit, watch, woolly hat). Grammar: too and not … enough; the Present passive (made of / used for / worn by). L2 dialogue: the new after-school athletics kit is too plain. L5 Book Club: a fairy tale — Prince Freddie, unhappy with his formal clothes and gold crown. L8 Culture: traditional clothes, including Jordanian dress, and festival clothing in Scotland and Guatemala (per the Activity Book cross-check). L9 English in action: shopping for clothes. L10–11 Literacy: information texts (skiing clothing/equipment labelling and writing). Project: a collage of traditional clothes; pros and cons of sports kits.
- **Unit 6 — Adventures (pp.18–27).** Vocabulary: outdoor equipment (bandage, blanket, camping stove, compass, first-aid kit, matches, penknife, plaster, rucksack, shelter, rope, sleeping mat) and injuries/accidents (break your arm, burn your hand, call an ambulance, cut your finger, fall over, have a pain/accident, hurt your ankle, keep cool/warm, lie down, take medicine). Grammar: indefinite pronouns (someone/something/anywhere, no one/nothing, everyone/everything); should/shouldn't, must/mustn't, need to/don't need to. L2 dialogue: Faisal back from a family camping trip in Wadi Mujib with a heavy, dirty rucksack. L5 Book Club: adventure story — Sami, Eman and their dad hiking in the Dana Nature Reserve, an accident on the last day. L8 Culture: Scouts and survival skills. L9 English in action: asking about injuries and illnesses. L10–11 Literacy: quizzes — reading and writing a first-aid quiz. Project: a list of interesting activities for a Scout group / a trip presentation.
- **Lara's Learning Club — Language booster 3 (pp.28–29)** and **Think like a scientist! How can we classify types of energy in diagrams? (pp.30–31)**: months, seasons, weather and telling the time booster; CLIL spread on energy types and diagram classification (machines turning electricity into heat and movement), with a T-chart/diagram practise lesson.
- **Unit 7 — Entertainment (pp.32–41).** Vocabulary: jobs in entertainment (animator, camera operator, clown, comedian, costume designer, make-up artist, performer, presenter, puppeteer, sound editor, stunt performer, writer) and places of entertainment (adventure playground, aquarium, art gallery, bowling alley, circus, ice rink, planetarium, safari park, science museum, soft play centre, theme park, water park). Grammar: Present perfect with since and for; Present continuous for future arrangements. L2 dialogue: Arlo's clown costume, had since he was small. L5 Book Club: a diary — a family holiday in Aqaba, the marine centre visit. L8 Culture: performers around the world (including Dabke and Cirque du Soleil per the Activity Book). L9 English in action: buying tickets. L10–11 Literacy: adverts — reading and writing an advert for a day out. Project: a poster for a performance / talent show.
- **Unit 8 — Awesome animals (pp.42–51).** Vocabulary: animal body parts (antennae, beak, claws, fin, flippers, paws, pouch, scales, spines, tongue, webbed feet, whiskers) and animal verbs (bite, die, dig, dive, feed on, fight, fly away, hide, lay eggs, roar, run away, sting). Grammar: deduction modals may, might, could, can't, must; the First conditional. L2 dialogue: Alia and Lara guessing an unseen animal in the wild. L5 Book Club: a folk tale — the animals argue over the best group; the platypus belongs to all of them. L8 Culture: national animals of different countries. L9 English in action: saying what you like and don't like. L10–11 Literacy: fact files — reading animal fact files (tiger sharks) and writing one. Project: a world map of national animals / rewriting a familiar story.
- **Lara's Learning Club — Language booster 4 (pp.52–53)** and **Think like a scientist! How can we make our carbon footprint smaller? (pp.54–55)**: travel items booster with going to and will for future; CLIL spread on greenhouse gases, causes and effects, and a T-chart practise lesson.
- **Festivals (pp.56–57).** Father's Day (Malak narrates, June) and World Oceans Day (Amer narrates, June; beach clean-up, water cycle).
- **Reference section (pp.58–61).** Grammar reference pp.58–59 (too/not enough and Present passive; indefinite pronouns and should/must/need to; Present perfect since/for and Present continuous for arrangements; deduction modals, First conditional, going to/will in the Learning Club); How to write… p.60 (an information text, a quiz, an advert, a fact file); English in action reference p.61 (shopping for clothes, asking about injuries, buying tickets, likes/dislikes); acknowledgements p.62.

## Grammar and vocabulary spine for the book

Grammar, in teaching order: Present perfect already/just/yet recycled (Welcome) → too/not enough + Present passive (U5) → indefinite pronouns + should/must/need to (U6) → Present perfect since/for + Present continuous for future arrangements (U7) → deduction modals (may/might/could/can't/must) + First conditional (U8), with going to/will added in Language booster 4. This is a clear step up from Semester 1: the passive, conditionals and modal deduction are the year's exit grammar. Vocabulary fields: clothes and description; outdoor equipment and injuries; entertainment jobs and venues; animal body parts and behaviour verbs.

## Honest negatives

- This file is a watermarked proof copy ("under preparation and checking"), not the final print PDF — file it as proof-state; a clean final Semester 2 pupil PDF is still worth obtaining.
- No answer key or audioscripts in the pupil book (Teacher's Book only); listening is track-referenced only.
- No wordlist in the pupil book — it is in the Semester 2 Activity Book (pp.50–55 there).
- Semester 2 assumes Semester 1: the Welcome unit only works as revision if Units 1–4 (Semester 1) have been taught; nothing here re-teaches relative pronouns, Past continuous or could/had to from scratch.
- The fairy tale, diary and folk tale are the only extended reading texts; there is no non-fiction book-length text and no poetry in this semester (Semester 1 had the Book Club poem).
