## The book itself

- Source file: `grade-07_gov_jordan_english-language_teacher_term-1.pdf` — 26,971,145 bytes; 140 PDF pages (pdfinfo).
- Identity verified from the rendered cover (PDF p.1), imprint (PDF p.2) and contents (PDF p.3). Cover: "Jordan TEAM Together, Grade 7, Semester 1 — Teacher's Book with Digital Resources" (teal cover, desert-and-skyline image). The filename's "term-1" is correct for this book — and, read carefully, this Teacher's Book is the record's only witness that was originally labelled Term 1. After the cover checks on its companions, the true shape is the reverse of the record's note: **all three Grade 7 books in hand are Semester 1** (pupil and activity files were misnamed term-2), this teacher book matches them unit for unit, and **Grade 7 Semester 2 has no books in the record at all** — no pupil book, no activity book, no teacher book.
- Why this book matters doubly: as the only Grade 7 book whose filename told the truth, it independently confirms the pupil/activity identity — its lesson notes teach City life, Our future, Let's read! and Our planet (Units 1–4), quote the same dialogues and give keys for the same pupil pages (e.g. the City life vocabulary key: luggage, police station, tour guide, fountain, tourists, clock tower, backpacker). If the pupil file were really Semester 2, this Semester 1 teacher book could not key it — it does, page for page.
- Author: Jenny Heath, "with Magdalena Custodio and Victoria Bewick" (contents page). Imprint asserts Heath's author rights; thanks Magdalena Custodio and Victoria Bewick.
- Publisher: The National Center for Curriculum Development, Amman. Adaptation Committee: Prof. Nayel Darweesh Al-Shara'h (Head); Dr. Manal Fahed Aburumman (Coordinator); Dr. Hanan Hasan El-Kanash; Saad Mohammad Odeh.
- Edition/ISBN: First edition (trial) © Pearson Education Limited and York Press Ltd. 2024; Second edition (revised and updated) © 2025. ISBN 978-9923-41-919-9 — the third of the consecutive trio (pupil …917-5, activity …918-2, teacher …919-9). National Library deposit 2025/3/1602. Board of Education decision No. 49/2024, adopted for 2025/2026. Printed 2024, reprinted 2025.
- Page offset: printed folio = PDF page − 2 (contents: Scope and sequence p.2 = PDF p.4; Glossary p.136). Pages below are the Teacher's Book's own printed pages. It is four pages shorter than the Grade 6 teacher books (140 vs 144 PDF pages) and its Glossary sits at p.136 rather than p.132.
- Citation check (20/20): p.2 Scope and sequence; p.4 Introduction; p.6 Course components; p.8 Unit walkthrough; p.11–12 Assessment / English Benchmark; p.18 Classroom language; p.19 Games bank; p.28 City life in the lesson notes (with the used to grammar teaching at the same spread range); p.30 "Fountain" in the City life vocabulary key; p.50 Our future in the notes; p.72 Language booster 1 notes; p.80 Let's read! in the notes; p.86 reported speech teaching; p.102 Our planet in the notes; p.124 Language booster 2 notes; p.132 World Teacher's Day notes; p.134 Mother's Day notes; p.136 Glossary.

## What the guide adds (scripts, keys, method)

- **Answer keys for every pupil and activity activity**, embedded in the lesson notes (the notes also mark where answers are "circled in red" on the facsimile pupil pages). Activity Book work is keyed inside the relevant lessons.
- **Audioscripts distributed through the notes**: notes repeatedly direct the teacher to a named page "for audioscript" for the longer listenings (e.g. "Play the audio. See page 48 for audioscript" in the City life lessons — the script pages sit inside the notes block), and the introduction promises answer keys and audioscripts as the Teacher's Book's core content. There is no single script appendix.
- **Front matter (pp.2–21)**: Scope and sequence (pp.2–3); Introduction (p.4 — course design, grammar step-by-step, the How to write… and Writing Tip support); Course components (p.6 — including tests: unit tests, **three** End-of-semester tests and one End-of-year test at this level, in Standard (A) and Challenge versions, plus the level posters built from unit vocabulary); Unit walkthrough (p.8); Assessment (p.11 — monitoring, formative techniques including the lollipop-stick technique named in the Welcome notes, English Benchmark); How to… (p.15 — projects, mixed-ability groups with the Bloom's-taxonomy framing, posters — the Grade 7 book has **eight** full-colour vocabulary posters); Classroom language (p.18); Games bank (p.19).
- **Lesson notes (from p.22)**: for every pupil lesson — objectives and aims, target language, a Presentation stage, staged procedures with timing-friendly steps, answer keys, Diversity Support/Challenge and Extra activity boxes, and Assessment for Learning notes. Unit Mapping pages link each unit to curriculum themes (the Grade 6 pattern).
- **Back matter**: Glossary (p.136 — unit wordlists with IPA for Units 1–4 and both Learning Clubs), closing notes and acknowledgements/image credits. The Grade 6 teacher books' separate "Extra resources" card-set section is not listed in this book's contents — the contents end at the Glossary (see Honest negatives).

## Full unit/lesson map

The notes track the Grade 7 Semester 1 Pupil's Book (pupil pp.4–61) lesson by lesson:

- **Welcome — Let's get started! (notes from TB p.22; pupil pp.4–7)**: keys for the blog/magazine premise (Team, Everyone, articles, find out…) and the About Us reading (team members Ghada, Dominic, Selina, Luisa… in the key), with the lollipop-stick monitoring technique used in the notes.
- **Unit 1 — City life (pupil pp.8–17)**: vocabulary presentation key (luggage, police station, tour guide; fountain, tourists, clock tower, backpacker); the Monument dialogue notes (reported answers in the key: "She said she thought it was brilliant"); used to grammar notes ("used to + verb on page 33 for answer key" cross-reference style); picture-story, cities Culture, travel-information and article-writing lessons.
- **Unit 2 — Our future (pupil pp.18–27)**: jobs/life-events presentation; will-prediction dialogue notes; poem Book Club; might/may/could lesson; university-degrees Culture; offers of help; Dalia's blog literacy lessons.
- **Dana's Learning Club — Language booster 1 + Social Studies (pupil pp.28–31)**: booster notes and the compare/contrast CLIL lessons (Fatima's mountain-cities report keyed).
- **Unit 3 — Let's read! (pupil pp.32–41)**: book-types presentation; survey dialogue; reported-statements grammar (the key converts the survey quotes — "They said that they enjoyed reading"); passive forms drawn from the Culture/mystery material (the tower was built…); mystery Book Club; famous-mysteries Culture; agreeing/disagreeing; detective-story literacy (The missing jewellery).
- **Unit 4 — Our planet (pupil pp.42–51)**: environment/extreme-weather presentation; competition dialogue; modals (should/must/need to) notes; diary Book Club; climate-danger Culture; giving advice; reports literacy (survey language, no-opinion rule).
- **Dana's Learning Club — Language booster 2 + Social Studies (pupil pp.52–55)**: reflexives booster; the rainforest-explanation CLIL (key: plants start dying, animals lose food and habitat, gases go into the air and cause pollution, so people can get ill; pollution causes global warming).
- **Festivals (pupil pp.56–57)**: World Teacher's Day notes (project: a group collage to thank a teacher) and Mother's Day notes (project: find out about Father's Day).
- **Glossary (TB p.136)** and credits.

## Grammar and vocabulary spine for the book

The Semester 1 Grade 7 spine — used to and future forms → will/might/may/could → reported statements and the passive → modals and reported questions/commands — presented as teachable steps with rule answers, script pages and differentiation, and tabulated in the Scope and sequence (TB pp.2–3) with the semester's phonics (hard/soft c; letter sounds str/spr/scr and tch; voiced/unvoiced th) and projects (city fact file, university-degree poster, unsolved-mystery presentation, environment action plan).

## Honest negatives

- This book teaches Semester 1 only. It contains no trace of Grade 7 Semester 2 (Units 5–8): a Semester 2 teacher book is missing from the record, along with the Semester 2 pupil and activity books.
- The tests it describes (unit tests, three end-of-semester tests, end-of-year test, Standard and Challenge versions) are not inside this PDF.
- Contents list no Extra resources/cards section (unlike the Grade 6 teacher books); the book ends at the Glossary plus notes/credits — the poster set is a separate physical component.
- The pupil and activity companions in the record are second-edition (2025) files while this teacher book's imprint also reads second edition 2025 — the trio is edition-consistent, unlike the Grade 6 set; but all three Grade 7 files were printed 2024/reprinted 2025, so they are the 2024 trial text in revised form, not a 2026 text.
- Audioscripts are scattered through the notes by cross-reference, not collected; extracting a clean script set requires page-by-page harvesting of the notes.

## The book itself

- Consequence for the record shape: the Grade 7 set in hand is a complete Semester 1 trio — Pupil's Book, Activity Book and Teacher's Book, all Semester 1 (the two "term-2" files are both Semester 1 books; the teacher file is correctly named term-1) — and **no Grade 7 Semester 2 book of any kind is in the record**. The earlier record note ("no Grade 7 Term 1 student book/workbook, no Term 2 teacher book") is exactly backwards.
- Series/authors: Jordan Team Together; Anna Osborn "with Kay Bentley" (contents page). The WOW! Magazine framing continues from Grade 6, but the team has a new member/presenter figure, Dana, whose name heads the Learning Club strand ("Dana's Learning Club" replaces "Lara's Learning Club").
- Publisher: The National Center for Curriculum Development, Amman. Adaptation Committee in Jordan — different from Grades 6 and 8: Prof. Nayel Darweesh Al-Shara'h (Head); Dr. Manal Fahed Aburumman (Coordinator); Dr. Hanan Hasan El-Kanash; Saad Mohammad Odeh.

Board of Education decision No. 47/2024 (7/5/2024 council meeting; 26/6/2024 board), adopted for 2025/2026. Printed 2024, reprinted 2025.
- Page offset: printed folio = PDF page − 2. Pages below are printed pages.
- Citation check (20/20, all on Semester 1 content): p.4 "Let's get started!" (Welcome); p.8 Unit 1 City life opener; p.10 the Monument in London dialogue question; p.12 picture-story Book Club; p.14 cities article (Culture); p.15 asking for travel information; p.18 Unit 2 Our future opener; p.20 professional-footballer dialogue; p.24 university-degrees Culture; p.28 Language booster 1; p.30 "How can we compare and contrast things?"; p.32 Unit 3 Let's read! opener; p.36 mystery-story Book Club; p.42 Unit 4 Our planet opener; p.46 diary Book Club; p.52 Language booster 2; p.54 "How can we give an explanation?"; p.56 World Teacher's Day festival; p.57 Mother's Day festival; p.58 reported speech in the grammar reference.

## Full unit/lesson map

- **Welcome — Let's get started! (pp.4–7).** The WOW! Team returns for a new year; the team introduces the magazine's new jobs and plans. Vocabulary: hobbies and interests revision. Lessons: the magazine/blog premise page and the About Us Team Talk (the team includes Dana, Mei, Alex, Sami and others).
- **Unit 1 — City life (pp.8–17).** Vocabulary: city tourism (capital city, backpacker, tour guide, tourist information centre, accommodation, luggage, statue, monument, police station, fountain, tourist, clock tower) and travel/transport (taxi, neighbourhood, lorry, roundabout, coach, road sign, railway station, traffic lights, crossroads, fire engine). Grammar: used to; Present continuous for future; Present simple for future (timetabled events). L2 dialogue: Dana and Mei; the Monument in London and what past event it remembers. L5 Book Club: a picture story. L8 Culture: an article about interesting cities of the world (big city vs small city). L9 English in action: asking for travel information (single/return tickets, times). L10–11 Literacy: articles — reading and writing an article about a place. Phonics/project: hard and soft c; a fact file about a city.
- **Unit 2 — Our future (pp.18–27).** Vocabulary: jobs (hairdresser, manager, dentist, computer programmer, inventor, architect, novelist, graphic designer, politician, diver, professional footballer, journalist) and life events (be born, go to school, get a degree, go to university, find a job, get married, have children, start a business, retire, grow up, move house, go backpacking). Grammar: will for predictions; might, may and could for predictions. L2 dialogue: Sami predicts Alex will become a professional footballer. L5 Book Club: a poem. L8 Culture: popular university degrees. L9 English in action: making and responding to offers of help. L10–11 Literacy: blogs — Dalia's blog (future jobs, robots and doctors) and writing a blog. Project: a poster about an unusual university degree.
- **Dana's Learning Club — Language booster 1 (pp.28–29)** and **Social Studies: How can we compare and contrast things? (pp.30–31)**: city-places booster with suggestions (Salwa and Heba's dialogue and weekend schedule); a Social Studies CLIL spread on comparison, practised on Fatima's report about two mountain cities (La Rinconada, Peru, 5,100 m, among them).
- **Unit 3 — Let's read! (pp.32–41).** Vocabulary: books (fantasy, graphic novel, recipe book, biography, science fiction, drama, adventure story, poetry, mystery, horror story, self-help, detective story) and story adjectives (tiny, dark, empty, steep, strange, gorgeous, terrible, secret, huge, bright, popular, charming). Grammar: Reported speech (statements); Present simple passive and Past simple passive. L2 dialogue: the girls' book survey (adventure, detective and mystery stories). L5 Book Club: a mystery story. L8 Culture: famous mysteries. L9 English in action: agreeing and disagreeing. L10–11 Literacy: detective stories — Nawal's "The missing jewellery" and writing a detective story. Project: a presentation about an unsolved mystery.
- **Unit 4 — Our planet (pp.42–51).** Vocabulary: the environment (protect the environment, climate change, save water, plant trees, switch off lights, waste energy, recycle, pollution, destroy the rainforests, pick up rubbish, use renewable energy, global warming) and extreme weather (hurricane, tornado, thunder and lightning, gale, heatwave, drought, forest fire, volcano, tsunami, flood, avalanche, snowstorm). Grammar: modal verbs should, must, mustn't, need to, needn't; Reported speech — Wh- and yes/no questions and commands. L2 dialogue: an environment competition and saving energy. L5 Book Club: a diary. L8 Culture: animals in danger from climate change. L9 English in action: giving advice. L10–11 Literacy: reports — reading survey reports and writing a report (no opinion, facts and survey language). Project: an action plan to help an endangered animal / environment action plan.
- **Dana's Learning Club — Language booster 2 (pp.52–53)** and **Social Studies: How can we give an explanation? (pp.54–55)**: reflexive pronouns booster (by myself/yourself… in dialogue); a Social Studies CLIL spread on explanation, practised on why rainforests are in danger and why cutting them down is a problem (fuel, burning, dying plants, soil).
- **Festivals (pp.56–57).** World Teacher's Day (Rola narrates; 5 October) and Mother's Day around the world (Omar narrates).
- **Reference section (pp.58–61).** Grammar reference (p.58: used to and future forms; will and might/may/could; passive and reported statements; modals and reported questions/commands); How to write… (p.59: an article, a blog, a good story, a report); English in action reference (p.60); acknowledgements (p.62 in the PDF's foliation).

## Grammar and vocabulary spine for the book

Grammar, in teaching order: used to + Present continuous for future + Present simple for future (U1) → will and might/may/could for prediction (U2) → reported statements + Present/Past simple passive (U3) → modals of obligation/advice (should, must, need to) + reported questions and commands (U4). This is the natural continuation of Grade 6 (the passive met in Grade 6 Unit 5 is extended; reported speech is new). Vocabulary fields: city and transport; jobs and life events; books and story adjectives; environment and extreme weather. The CLIL strand changes from Grade 6's science ("Think like a scientist!") to Social Studies (comparing/contrasting; giving explanations).

## Honest negatives

- **This is not a Semester 2 book.** Any course plan that treats this file as Grade 7 Term 2 content will double-teach Semester 1 and leave Semester 2 (Units 5–8 of Grade 7) untaught. Grade 7 Semester 2 pupil, activity and teacher books are all missing from the record and still need to be found.
- No answer key or audioscripts in the pupil book (Teacher's Book only); listening is track-referenced.
- No wordlist in the pupil book — it is in the Activity Book (pp.50–53 there).
- The book assumes the Grade 6 WOW! Magazine world but renames the Learning Club presenter (Dana, not Lara); a reader tracking characters across grades should note the change.
- Nothing in this file covers Grade 7 Semester 2 festivals or grammar; its festivals are World Teacher's Day and Mother's Day only.

## Full unit/lesson map

- **Unit 1 — The things we do (pp.4–13).** Vocabulary: daily routines (pp.4–5), free-time activities and frequency expressions (p.6), phrasal verbs (pp.8–9), verbs and their prepositions (p.11). Grammar: Present Simple affirmative and negative (p.5); adverbs of frequency and Present Simple questions (p.7). Pronunciation: plural -s. Reading (pp.8–9): "Stereotypes! Are they true?" — Active Reading: understanding the main idea. Listening (p.11): a podcast about what someone likes to do. Speaking (p.10): talking about likes and dislikes. Writing (pp.12–13): a personal profile (model: Dana, 14, from Jordan; linking with and/but/so/because). Viewing/presenting: deciding on a present.
- **Unit 2 — No place like home (pp.14–23).** Vocabulary: house (pp.14–15), rooms and furniture (p.16), household chores (p.17), adjectives and their opposites (pp.18–19), collocations with do and make (p.20). Grammar: There is/There are with some and any (pp.14–15); can/can't (p.17). Reading (pp.18–19): "What makes your house a home?" — Active Reading: predicting; the grammar spread works from a text by Dalia, a wheelchair user, on home accessibility. Listening (p.20): an interview about roommates. Speaking (p.21): asking for information (renting a holiday flat dialogue). Writing (pp.22–23): a description of a place (model: Ghada's bedroom post).
- **Life Skills — How to avoid time wasters (pp.24–25).** A study-skills website text (Fadi, Laila and Ziad's study problems) and a free-hours calculation task.
- **Unit 3 — Eat in – eat out (pp.26–35).** Vocabulary: food and drink (pp.26–27), food products, containers and prices (p.28), jobs and kitchen items (p.31), phrasal verbs (pp.32–33). Grammar: countable/uncountable nouns with some/any/no (pp.26–27); quantifiers — a lot of, too many, too much, a few, a little, not many, not much (p.30). Pronunciation: weak form of of. Reading (pp.32–33): "Ethical restaurants". Listening (p.31): a radio interview about food / food photography strand. Speaking (p.29): ordering food (a cheese-sandwich dialogue with stock problems). Writing (pp.34–35): a restaurant review (models by Ramzi and Nasser; the praised restaurant serves British food).
- **Unit 4 — School life (pp.36–45).** Vocabulary: school (pp.36–37), school subjects and classroom objects (p.38), education collocations (pp.40–41). Grammar: Present Continuous (pp.36–37); Present Simple vs Present Continuous (p.39), worked through a "No Pens Day" text. Pronunciation: syllable stress. Reading (pp.40–41): "App development at a school" by Abeer Alqadi — The Lab in Boston, where students mix lessons with real app work. Listening (p.42): a podcast about commuting / unusual journeys to school. Speaking (p.43): asking for, giving and refusing permission (Muna and Nada borrowing dialogue). Writing (pp.44–45): an internet forum post; the reading strand includes Mariam, a secondary student in Amman, describing a typical day at an international school in Jordan.
- **Life Skills — How to manage exam stress (pp.46–47).** Zeina Alsa'ati of the Student Support Centre on coping with exam stress; stress-ranking and strategy tasks.
- **Unit 5 — Appearances (pp.48–57).** Vocabulary: personality adjectives (pp.48–49), appearance and clothes (p.50), family and lookalikes (pp.54–55). Grammar: Past Simple of be and can, and Past Simple affirmative of regular and irregular verbs (pp.48–49, via a text about the narrator's neighbour Ghada and a Maha-and-Grandma conversation); Past Simple negative and question forms (p.52). Pronunciation: -ed endings. Reading (pp.54–55): "Family Lookalikes" (genes and resemblance). Listening (p.53): an interview about clothes and the environment. Speaking (p.51): shopping for clothes (Adel and Hani). Writing (pp.56–57): an informal email (model from Imad, about a new girl, Randa, from Balqa).
- **Culture Spot (pp.58–59).** Food from around the world — Ziad, a Jordanian blogger, answers questions about dishes from four countries.
- **Literature Spot (pp.60–61).** An adapted extract from Jules Verne's Journey to the Centre of the Earth (the parchment scene), with listening and discussion.
- **Back matter.** Word List (pp.62–66, per unit/lesson with IPA and audio references WL1.1…); Grammar Reference (pp.67–71, keyed to lesson and page); Irregular Verbs (p.72); Communication (pp.73–75, Student A/B pair-work pages for Units 2–3 exercises — restaurant menus, flat tasks); Acknowledgements (final printed page).

## Grammar and vocabulary spine for the book

Grammar: Present Simple (affirmative/negative/questions) + adverbs of frequency (U1) → There is/are with some/any + can/can't (U2) → countable/uncountable nouns with some/any/no + quantifiers (U3) → Present Continuous and Present Simple vs Continuous (U4) → Past Simple (be/can; affirmative; negative/questions) (U5). Deliberately a re-foundation year: High Note Semester 1 re-teaches the present-tense system and articles/quantifiers before the Past Simple, at a higher text level than Team Together — it does not assume the Grade 6–7 grammar (passive, reported speech) and does not use it. Vocabulary fields: routines and free time; home and furniture; food, containers, prices and food jobs; school and education collocations; appearance, clothes and personality.

## Honest negatives

- **Record shape for Grade 8:** this Student's Book and its Workbook are the only Grade 8 books in the record — there are **no Grade 8 Semester 2 books and no Grade 8 teacher books at all** in hand. Nothing here can key the Workbook or supply audioscripts.
- The Communication pages (pp.73–75) and two blank PDF pages near the end (PDF pp.78–79) are apparatus/blank, not missing units: the contents map is complete at five units.
- No answer key in the Student's Book; the Workbook's only key is for its Revision sections.
- The series break matters for course building: no WOW! Team, no Learning Club, no festivals strand, and the lesson code system changes (1A–7A lessons, B lessons in the Workbook). Citations to Grade 6–7 conventions do not transfer.
- Semester 1 only: Units 6+ of High Note (and any Semester 2 grammar beyond the Past Simple) are not in this book.

## Full unit/lesson map

- **Unit 1 — The things we do (pp.4–11).** Lesson 1B Vocabulary and Grammar: Present Simple affirmative/negative (starred form-choice and sentence-building, e.g. family breakfast routines). Lesson 2B Vocabulary: free-time activities and frequency expressions (Samar's calendar). Lesson 3B Grammar: adverbs of frequency (ordering the adverbs; "three times a week" frequency phrases). Lesson 6B Vocabulary/Pronunciation: Active Pronunciation — the final -s in plural nouns. **Self-assessment (p.9)** and **Revision (pp.10–11)**: vocabulary/grammar completion plus a Use of English multiple-choice text (a volleyball match notice).
- **Unit 2 — No place like home (pp.12–19).** Lesson 1B: There is/There are with some and any (a flat-for-sale text; a Barcelona holiday text). Lesson 2B Vocabulary: rooms and furniture (letter-gap completion: wardrobe, sofa, shower, mirror). Lesson 3B Grammar and Vocabulary: can/can't (notes-to-sentences; word-order questions, "Can you speak French?"). Lesson 5B Vocabulary: vocabulary extension and pronunciation (silent h words; question words). **Self-assessment (p.17)** and **Revision (pp.18–19)**: room definitions, there is/are completion, and a Use of English text ("Flat in Irbid" — four bedrooms, a new kitchen).
- **Unit 3 — Eat in – eat out (pp.20–27).** Lesson 1B: countable/uncountable nouns with some/any/no (a/an/some/any/no dialogue completion). Lesson 2B Vocabulary: food products, containers, prices (vowel-gap container names — tin, can…; a shopping total of 5.31 JOD). Lesson 4B Grammar: quantifiers (a lot of, too many, too much, a few, a little, not many, not much), with a ★★★ Use of English gap-fill. Lesson 5B Vocabulary: jobs (vowel-gap: waiter…) and pronunciation. **Self-assessment (p.25)** and **Revision (pp.26–27)**: countable/uncountable table, sentence choice, and a Use of English text about a restaurant called The Health Kitchen.
- **Unit 4 — School life (pp.28–35).** Lesson 1B: Present Continuous (word order; mini-conversations; picture sentences — Mrs Allaham drinking coffee). Lesson 2B Vocabulary: school subjects and classroom objects (letter-gap subjects; calculator, dictionary…). Lesson 3B Grammar: Present Simple and Present Continuous contrasted (the National Olive Festival text — a once-a-year event vs today). Lesson 5B Vocabulary/Pronunciation: Active Pronunciation — the /iː/ sound and its ea/ee/ey spellings. **Self-assessment (p.33)** and **Revision (pp.34–35)**: definitions matching, Present Continuous forms, and a Use of English text (a Sports Day notice; what Nour borrows from Alia).
- **Unit 5 — Appearances (pp.36–43).** Lesson 1B: Past Simple of be and can (was/were/could completion; a text built from the verbs box). Lesson 2B Vocabulary: appearance and clothes (boots, cardigan, coat, hat, jeans, shoes, skirt, top, tracksuit bottoms…). Lesson 4B Grammar: Past Simple negative and question forms (short answers; correcting sentences from an information table — Raed, Dalia, Zeinab, Faten, Ghada, Nadia, Hamed's weekend activities). Lesson 5B Vocabulary/Pronunciation: /s/ and /ʃ/ sounds. **Self-assessment (p.41)** and **Revision (pp.42–43)**: word-box completion, was/were/could forms, and a biographical Use of English text (Steve Jobs, born 24 February 1955; the Romans' arrival).
- **Reference back matter (pp.44–49).** Phrasal verbs (pp.44–45: eat in/eat out, and preposition patterns — congratulations on, agree with, a conversation about…); Times and dates (p.46: days of the week, ordinal numbers to 90th); Word building (p.47: opposite-meaning prefixes, un-…) and a Pronunciation table (consonant symbols with example words); **Revision Answer Key (pp.48–49)** for all five units' Revision sections.

## Grammar and vocabulary spine for the book

Practice spine = the High Note Semester 1 spine: Present Simple and frequency (U1) → there is/are + some/any and can/can't (U2) → countability and quantifiers (U3) → Present Continuous and the present-tense contrast (U4) → Past Simple in all forms (U5). Its distinctive apparatus is assessment: a per-unit Self-assessment page keyed to Student's Book pages, a per-unit Revision with exam-style Use of English texts, and the course's only printed answer key — for those Revision sections only.

## Honest negatives

- Only the Revision sections have answers in this book; the B-lesson exercises have no key here (a teacher book would hold them, and **no Grade 8 teacher book is in the record**).
- **Record shape for Grade 8:** Student's Book + Workbook, Semester 1 only. No Semester 2 books and no teacher books — the Workbook's listening exercises (e.g. tracks 2.7, 3.12, 4.10, 5.10) have no scripts anywhere in the held Grade 8 set.
- The B-lesson numbering is not complete: units practise selected B lessons (1B–6B in Unit 1, 1B–5B in Units 2–5, with some numbers skipped in print, e.g. no 4B/6B spreads in later units) — this mirrors the Student's Book's A-lesson selection and is not missing pages; the contents page (printed p.3) confirms the intended span per unit.
- Image credits are bound at the front of the PDF (PDF p.4) rather than the back — a binding/export quirk of this file, worth noting so nobody files it as a mis-ordered scan.
- The final printed content is the Revision Answer Key (pp.48–49); the two trailing PDF pages are blank.

## Full unit/lesson map
- **Unit 1 Looking good (pp.4–13).** Grammar: Present Simple and Continuous, state and action verbs (1A, pp.4–5); Articles (5A, p.10); pronunciation /ðə/ and /ðiː/. Vocabulary: verb phrases with *dress* (pp.4–5), appearance/clothes/footwear/accessories — "My Wardrobe" (2A, p.6), facial features (3A, p.7), phrasal verbs (4A, pp.8–9). Reading pp.8–9: *The power of appearance* — article on how clothes and glasses change how others perceive us (summary). Listening p.7: podcast about jobs in the entertainment industry. Speaking p.11: participating in conversations. Writing pp.12–13: an informal email — model is a prize "star letter" from Huda in Amman, a sales assistant in a Jordanian women's clothing shop, with Dalia's reply.
- **Unit 2 The digital mind (pp.14–23).** Grammar: Present Perfect Simple and Continuous (1A, pp.14–15); verb patterns (4A, p.19). Vocabulary: scientific research (pp.14–15), science and phrases with *think* and *mind* (3A, p.18), uses of drones (5A, p.20), health and computers (pp.22–23). Reading pp.16–17: *Science fiction or science fact?* — article on super-intelligent machines and whether lifelike AI could become real (summary); Active Reading: skimming and scanning. Listening p.20: interview about drones. Speaking p.21: making choices. Writing pp.22–23: a blog post — Zeinab's blog "Switch it off!" arguing screens are bad for the brain, with reader comments.
- **Life Skills (pp.24–25): How to give a presentation** — structuring (beginning/middle/end), signposting language, delivery practice.
- **Unit 3 Active and healthy (pp.26–35).** Grammar: Past Simple, Past Continuous and Past Perfect (1A, pp.26–27); *used to* and *would* (4A, p.30). Vocabulary: sports collocations (pp.26–27), sports/activities/fitness/exercise (2A, p.28), injuries, accidents and emergencies (3A, p.29), diet and nutrition (6A, pp.32–33). Reading pp.32–33: *Say 'yum' to healthy eating!* — article asking whether school meals are healthy enough, with a "superfoods" fact box (summary). Listening p.29: conversations about accidents; Active Listening: identifying the speaker's purpose. Speaking p.31: being polite; pronunciation: linking. Writing pp.34–35: a short story (a surfer and a lifeguard incident), with linker grouping.
- **Unit 4 Time to move (pp.36–45).** Grammar: modal and related verbs (1A, pp.36–37); defining and non-defining relative clauses (4A, p.41). Vocabulary: air travel (pp.36–37), holiday phrases (2A, pp.38–39), travel essentials and travel phrases (3A, p.40), city environments (5A, p.42). Reading pp.38–39: *How to have the perfect family holiday* — a family-holiday blog post (summary); Active Reading: identifying the author's attitudes. Listening p.42: radio programme about urban transport and pollution (incl. a bike lane in Amsterdam, air pollution in India). Speaking p.43: agreeing and disagreeing. Writing pp.44–45: a formal email of enquiry (outdoor-activity courses in Scotland).
- **Life Skills (pp.46–47): How to take part in a debate** — debating-club rules, for/against language.
- **Unit 5 The next step (pp.48–57).** Grammar: talking about the future (1A, pp.48–49); Future Continuous and Future Perfect (3A, pp.50–51). Vocabulary: personality adjectives (pp.48–49), phrasal verbs related to studying (2A, p.50), work and jobs (5A, p.53), future jobs (6A, pp.54–55). Reading pp.54–55: *The future of work* — article on how the workforce is changing (summary). Listening p.53: radio programme about the gig economy; Active Listening: taking notes. Speaking p.52: describing strengths and weaknesses (internship interviews — Jack and Asma). Writing pp.56–57: a personal statement as part of a university application (model by Mariam), with a 7-point tips box.
- **Culture Spot (pp.58–59): Tea culture around the world** — Japanese tea, Tibetan po cha, boba, Moroccan tea and maté, with listening and comparison tasks.
- **Literature Spot (pp.60–61): *The Time Machine* by H. G. Wells (1895)** — extract, filed as summary only: the Time Traveller shows his dinner guests (including the Psychologist) a small shiny model time machine and argues for time travel; the guests are sceptical and silent.
- **Back matter:** Word List pp.62–66 (unit-by-unit, with phonetics and audio codes WL1.1–WL5.5); Grammar Reference pp.67–71 (one spread per grammar point above); Irregular Verbs p.72; Communication appendix pp.73–75 (Student A/B pair tasks); Acknowledgements p.76.

## Grammar and vocabulary spine for the book
Grammar: present tenses + articles → present perfect tenses + verb patterns → past narrative tenses + *used to/would* → modals + relative clauses → future forms incl. Future Continuous/Perfect. Vocabulary: appearance & clothes → science/technology & the mind → sport, health & food → travel & cities → personality, study & work. Writing genres: informal email, blog post, short story, formal email of enquiry, personal statement. Exam-style strategies are taught through Active Reading/Listening boxes (skimming/scanning, author's attitude, speaker's purpose, note-taking).

## Honest negatives
- No separate answer key in the Student's Book (answers live in the Teacher's Book); audio is referenced by track numbers (e.g. 2.14) but no audio ships inside the PDF.
- The contents page's skills grid (PDF p.5) and the unit contents (PDF p.4) occasionally give slightly different lesson page spans than the lesson folios themselves; the folios were followed for this filing.
- This is a "first edition (trial)" — the imprint itself labels it a trial edition for 2025/2026.

## The book itself
- Verified from rendered cover/title/imprint pages: **Jordan High Note, Grade 10, Semester 1, Teacher's Book**. Publisher: National Center for Curriculum Development, Amman; © Pearson Education Limited and York Press Ltd. 2025.

Read whole via `pdftotext -layout` (the notes embed the full Student's Book/Workbook content references, audioscripts and answers); 18/18 citation anchors passed.

## What the guide adds (scripts, keys, method)
- **Introduction (printed pp.4–19):** what Jordan High Note is; the methodology (Active Reading/Active Listening strategies, Watch Out! boxes, Think Back recycling, critical thinking); key concepts; course components; a full **unit walkthrough**; teaching pathways; inclusive/mixed-ability guidance; **how to teach for exams** with Jordan High Note (the Revision and Use of English strands are the exam-prep route; self-study resources are listed); how to flip the classroom.
- **Teacher's Notes (pp.20–115):** for every unit, a Mapping page (scope and sequence, learning outcomes, performance indicators), then lesson-by-lesson notes for **both** the Student's Book (A) lessons and the Workbook (B) lessons, including the Revision lessons. Each lesson note gives: Overview/objectives, staged procedure with minute timings (Lead-in, Presentation, Practice, Production, Wrap-up), interaction codes (T–S, S–S, S–T), **answers and suggested answers inline**, **full audioscripts printed at the point of use**, Contingency Plan boxes (extra support/extension), and homework suggestions.
- **Culture Spot notes (pp.116–117)** and **Literature Spot notes (pp.118–119)** with the same mapping + procedure format; **Culture Notes (pp.120–125)** — background briefings tied to lessons (e.g. wood-fired ovens, BMX, drones, Oregon, applying to UK universities through UCAS).
- **Class Debates (p.124)** — a five-step method for running a class debate; **Debating: useful language (p.125)** — presenting, certainty, agreement/disagreement phrases.
- **Photocopiable Resources 1–22 (pp.126–161):** teacher's notes and answer keys first, then the worksheets — one per key lesson, e.g. R1 "Celebration time" (present tenses), R2 "Sketch artists" (appearance), R3 "Backstage" (listening, audio-script-based), R4 "Put on your thinking cap!" (articles), R5 "Space convention" (present perfect), R6 "Back to the moon" (skimming/scanning), R8 "Verb pattern battleships", R9 "Drone Boy", R10 "Ali – a football genius" (past tenses), R12 "There's been an accident!", R14 "Vietnam: know before you go" (modals), R17 "Match and combine" (relative clauses), R18 "The air we breathe", R22 "The changing world of work".
- **Graphic Organisers 1–5 (pp.162–168):** writing planners for the informal email, blog post, short story, formal email of enquiry and personal statement.

## Full unit/lesson map
The notes follow the Student's Book unit-for-unit: Unit 1 Looking good (notes from p.20), Unit 2 The digital mind, Unit 3 Active and healthy, Unit 4 Time to move, Unit 5 The next step (notes to p.115, incl. "05 Revision (WB)" at printed p.114), then Culture/Literature Spot notes, culture notes, debates, photocopiables and graphic organisers as above. Grammar/vocabulary content per unit is identical to the Student's Book filing (g10-student-term-1.md); the Teacher's Book adds procedure, scripts and keys, not new language content.

## Grammar and vocabulary spine for the book
Same spine as the Student's Book (present tenses → present perfect → past tenses → modals/relative clauses → future forms). The guide's added spine is pedagogical: mapping tables tie every lesson to curriculum learning outcomes, and the exam-teaching section ties the Revision/Use of English tasks to exam technique.

## Honest negatives
- There is **no separate test/exam bank and no end-of-book audioscript or workbook answer-key section** in this Teacher's Book — scripts and keys exist only inline inside the lesson notes (a reader looking for an appendix will not find one).
- Folio numbering stumbles around section dividers in the photocopiable section (divider pages are counted differently), so contents-page numbers and folios differ by one in places.
- The introduction's exam section points to resources outside this book (self-study/digital components) that are not included in the PDF.

## Full unit/lesson map
- **Unit 6 Do the right thing (pp.4–13).** Grammar: first and second conditionals (1A, pp.4–5); zero conditional and alternatives to *if* (4A, p.9). Vocabulary: dos and don'ts of using a mobile phone in public (pp.4–5), communicating (2A, pp.6–7), emotions (3A, p.8), relationships and caring for others (p.9). Reading pp.6–7: *If we kept it, we'd be rich* — story about a family that finds something valuable and argues over honesty vs keeping it (summary). Listening p.8: radio programme about being rich. Speaking p.10: asking for, giving and reacting to advice. Writing pp.12–13: a for-and-against essay (mobile phones in public).
- **Life Skills (pp.14–15): How to set SMART goals** — Specific/Measurable/Achievable/Relevant/Time-bound goal-setting with a diagram task.
- **Unit 7 In the spotlight (pp.16–25).** Grammar: reported speech (1A, pp.16–17); reported questions (5A, p.22). Vocabulary: TV news (pp.16–17), viewing habits (2A, p.18), success and failure (p.19), describing visual arts (4A, pp.20–21); pronunciation: syllable stress. Reading pp.20–21: *What is and isn't art?* (summary). Listening p.19: radio programme about promoting your work on social media (artist Paige). Speaking p.23: describing a personal experience. Writing pp.24–25: a review of a play (model review of "Our Comedy of Errors").
- **Unit 8 The evolution of advertising (pp.26–35).** Grammar: the passive (1A, pp.26–27); *have/get something done* (6A, p.33). Vocabulary: advertising (pp.26–27), spending habits (2A, p.28), money (3A, p.29), payments, trading and banking (4A, pp.30–31). Reading pp.30–31: *The way we pay* — history of money and payment methods (summary); Active Reading: understanding links in a text. Listening p.28: podcast about saving and spending money. Speaking p.32: complaints. Writing pp.34–35: an opinion essay.
- **Life Skills (pp.36–37): How to be more creative** — creativity techniques for schoolwork and essays.
- **Unit 9 The power of nature (pp.38–47).** Grammar: third conditional (1A, pp.38–39); *I wish/If only* for regrets (4A, p.42). Vocabulary: water and the ocean (pp.38–39), natural disasters and dealing with them (2A, p.40), environmental responsibility (3A, p.41), urban and rural life (6A, pp.44–45), sustainable homes (pp.46–47). Reading pp.44–45: *How a wildlife documentary changed my life* (summary), plus a text on Dana village in Jordan's Dana Biosphere Reserve (6A). Listening p.40: interview about surviving an earthquake. Speaking p.43: expressing and responding to regrets; pronunciation: unstressed *have*. Writing pp.46–47: an article for an ecology website (model by Amer); Active Reading: summarising texts.
- **Unit 10 Food for thought (pp.48–57).** Grammar: modal verbs for speculating about the present (1A, pp.48–49); modal verbs for speculating about the past (3A, p.51). Vocabulary: food tastes (p.48), ways of cooking and cooking equipment (2A, p.50), dining and restaurants (4A, pp.52–53), healthy eating (6A, p.55). Reading pp.52–53: *Dining with a difference!* — article on four unusual restaurants (summary). Listening p.55: radio discussion about healthy food and nutrition; Active Listening: facts, opinions and speculation. Speaking p.54: comparing and contrasting photographs. Writing pp.56–57: a formal letter (letter to the editor about ethical eating).
- **Life Skills (pp.58–59): How to identify fake news** — checking stories across sources; sample items include a Dutch teen inventor's ocean clean-up project.
- **Culture Spot (pp.60–61): The influence of bagpipes** — Scottish bagpipes and their spread/influence.
- **Literature Spot (pp.62–63): *The Mystery of Edwin Drood* by Charles Dickens** — extract, summary only: Mr Jasper, the cathedral choirmaster, in a moonlit scene connected with the old building; the novel is Dickens's unfinished mystery.
- **Back matter:** Word List pp.64–68; Grammar Reference pp.69–73; Irregular Verbs p.74; Communication pp.75–76; Acknowledgements.

## Grammar and vocabulary spine for the book
Conditionals (zero/first/second → third + *wish/if only*) frame the year-end grammar, with reported speech, the passive (+ causative *have/get*), and modal speculation. Vocabulary: ethics/communication → media & art → money & advertising → nature & environment → food & dining. Writing: for-and-against essay, play review, opinion essay, article, formal letter.

## Full unit/lesson map
- **Unit 1 Get the message (pp.4–15).** Grammar: present and past tenses review (1A, pp.4–5); question tags and echo questions (4A, p.9); pronunciation: intonation patterns. Vocabulary: collocations with *contact, message, touch* (pp.4–5), idioms and phrases for communication (3A, p.8), emotion adjectives (5A, p.10). Reading pp.6–7: *Making contact* — article on communicating with animals and what it would mean (summary); Active Reading: identifying the author's opinion. Listening p.10: radio interview about emotional intelligence. Speaking p.11: fixed phrases to express emotions. Writing pp.12–13: an analytical essay (will computer technology change how children communicate?). Revision pp.14–15, incl. reading *The next lingua franca* — on the future of English as a world language (summary).
- **Unit 2 Looking ahead (pp.16–27).** Grammar: future forms for predictions (1A, pp.16–17); future forms for plans and hopes (6A, p.23). Vocabulary: cause-and-effect phrases (p.17), threats to the environment — compound nouns (2A, p.18), weather vocabulary (p.19), endangered species (4A, p.20); pronunciation: stress in compound nouns. Reading pp.20–21: *They need saving too!* — article on endangered species and common myths about animals (summary). Listening p.19: a lecture about climate change; Active Listening: signposting. Speaking p.22: phrases for indecision, agreement/disagreement and reaching a decision. Writing pp.24–25: a formal email (Farid's complaint to Customer Service about sea plastic). Revision pp.26–27 (exam listening tasks).
- **Life Skills (pp.28–29): How to give a persuasive presentation** (Jameela's talk as model).
- **Unit 3 Influences (pp.30–41).** Grammar: past and present habits (1A, pp.30–31); relative and participle clauses (5A, p.36). Vocabulary: phrases for ways of behaving (pp.30–31), life events (2A, p.32), personal qualities and behaviour (3A, p.33); pronunciation: stress patterns in adjectives. Reading pp.34–35: a **summary of *Oliver Twist*** by Charles Dickens — orphan Oliver, the workhouse, running to London, Fagin's gang of child pickpockets, the handkerchief incident and Mr Brownlow (plot summary only). Listening p.32: radio programme about life events; Active Listening: facts vs opinion. Speaking p.37: generalisations. Writing pp.38–39: an opinion essay (families and quality time); Active Writing: topic sentences. Revision pp.40–41 + Use of English (text about school friend Mariam Sabah — people don't change much).
- **Unit 4 Inside story (pp.42–53).** Grammar: narrative tenses; Past Perfect Simple and Continuous (1A, pp.42–43); negative inversion (3A, p.45); pronunciation: weak forms. Vocabulary: fake news stories (pp.42–43), news reporting (2A, p.44), photography (5A, p.48); noun suffixes. Reading pp.46–47: two opposing news reports on a proposed hotel — *New hotel is just what the area needs* vs *New hotel, but no new hope for locals*; Active Reading: recognising bias. Listening p.48: radio programme about famous photos. Speaking p.49: telling an anecdote. Writing pp.50–51: a story ("The man in black"). Revision pp.52–53 + Use of English: *The death of the print media?*
- **Life Skills (pp.54–55): How to be good at debating.**
- **Unit 5 Making sense of the senses (pp.56–67).** Grammar: gerunds and infinitives (1A, pp.56–57); verbs with gerunds and infinitives (3A, p.59); *used to / be used to / get used to*. Vocabulary: the senses (2A, pp.56–58) incl. reading *The myth of the five senses* (humans have more than five senses — summary), social work and education (pp.60–61), food and cooking (5A, p.62). Reading pp.60–61: *Sabriye Tenberken: An amazing woman* — profile of the blind German social worker who founded a school for blind children in Tibet (Braille Without Borders), teaching Braille and life skills (summary); Active Reading: making inferences. Listening p.62: podcast about the history of ketchup. Speaking p.63: talking about preferences. Writing pp.64–65: a review (Osama's review of the Jordan Museum). Revision pp.66–67 (reading on gastronomy and a Yale food study).
- **Culture Spot (pp.68–69): The influence of Shakespeare** — words and phrases Shakespeare coined that are still used, his global reach; Ben Jonson's "not of an age, but for all time" line.
- **Literature Spot (pp.70–71): *Wuthering Heights* by Emily Brontë** — two extracts, filed as summary only: life on the Yorkshire moors at Wuthering Heights and Thrushcross Grange, Heathcliff and Catherine's intense bond, and the household's harsh atmosphere as seen by the narrator.
- **Back matter:** Word List pp.72–76 (with "Remember More" sections); Grammar Reference and Practice pp.77–89 (reference plus practice exercises for every grammar point, incl. sentence-transformation/Use of English style tasks); Irregular Verbs p.90; Use of English pp.91–93; Communication pp.94–96; Acknowledgements (Remember More and Use of English sections written by Joanna Sosnowska).

## Grammar and vocabulary spine for the book
A revision-and-extension year: tense system review → future forms → habits + relative/participle clauses → narrative tenses + negative inversion → gerunds/infinitives. Vocabulary is thematic-abstract (communication, environment, influence/behaviour, news/media, the senses). Writing: analytical essay, formal email, opinion essay, story, review. Every unit closes with Revision + Use of English in exam format.

## Honest negatives
- This Student's Book is the **2024 first edition**; its matching Workbook is the 72-page first-edition workbook (ISBN …619-8 — see g11-workbook-term-1-9667.md). A different, shortened "Second Edition, Revised and Updated" Workbook (2025) also circulates under this grade/semester (see g11-workbook-term-1.md) — the two workbooks must not be conflated.
- The contents page routes "Use of English" to pp.91–93 while short Use of English tasks also appear inside unit Revisions — both exist; the contents only signposts the appendix.
- No answer key or audio inside the PDF; answers are in the Teacher's Book.

- **Comparison with the 72-page first-edition Workbook (file "…-9667", ISBN 978-9923-41-619-8, 2024): they are different editions of the same book** — full verdict and evidence in g11-workbook-term-1-9667.md. In short: this edition keeps 4–5 B lessons per unit, drops the reading/speaking/writing B lessons and the end-of-book vocabulary-practice/self-check block, and inserts the Student's Book Revision spreads per unit.
- **Copy marking:** pages carry the diagonal Arabic "under preparation and review" watermark, including one whole watermark-only final page.

## Full unit/lesson map
- **Unit 1 Get the message (pp.4–11).** 1B Vocabulary and Grammar — Present and past tenses (pp.4–5); 3B Vocabulary — Communication idioms (p.6); 4B Grammar — Question tags and echo questions (p.7); 5B Vocabulary extension + Active Pronunciation: omitting /t/ in question tags (p.8). Self-assessment p.9. Revision pp.10–11 (incl. reading *The next lingua franca*).
- **Unit 2 Looking ahead (pp.12–19).** 1B — Future forms for predictions (pp.12–13); 2B Vocabulary — Threats to the environment (wordsearch) (p.14); 3B Vocabulary extension + Active Pronunciation: /iː/ and /ɪ/ (p.15); 6B Grammar — Future forms for plans and hopes (p.16). Self-assessment p.17. Revision pp.18–19 (incl. exam listening tasks).
- **Unit 3 Influences (pp.20–27).** 1B — Past and present habits (pp.20–21); 2B Vocabulary extension (p.22); 3B Vocabulary — Personal qualities and behaviour (p.23); 5B Grammar — Relative and participle clauses (p.24). Self-assessment p.25. Revision pp.26–27 (incl. Use of English text about Mariam Sabah).
- **Unit 4 Inside story (pp.28–35).** 1B — Narrative tenses (pp.28–29); 2B Vocabulary — News reporting (p.30); 3B Grammar — Negative inversion (p.31); 5B Vocabulary extension — photography words (p.32). Self-assessment p.33. Revision pp.34–35 (incl. Use of English: *The death of the print media?*).
- **Unit 5 Making sense of the senses (pp.36–43).** 1B — Gerunds and infinitives (pp.36–37); 2B Vocabulary — The senses (p.38); 3B Grammar — Verbs with gerunds and infinitives (p.39); 5B Vocabulary extension + Pronunciation: suffixes (p.40). Self-assessment p.41. Revision pp.42–43 (incl. reading "Food and science" — the art and science of choosing and preparing food).
- **Appendices:** Phrasal Verbs p.44; Prepositions pp.45–46; Word Building + Pronunciation Table p.47; Revision Answer Key pp.48–49.

## Grammar and vocabulary spine for the book
Same syllabus as the Student's Book and the first-edition Workbook (tenses review + question tags; future forms; habits + relative/participle clauses; narrative tenses + negative inversion; gerunds/infinitives), but practised through fewer, shorter lessons plus the Student's Book's own Revision material.

## Honest negatives
- **Contents-page error:** Unit 4's Revision is listed as "26–27" — those are Unit 3's Revision pages; Unit 4's Revision actually sits at pp.34–35 (verified on the pages themselves).
- The Revision spreads duplicate the Student's Book Revision pages verbatim (same texts and tasks), so this workbook adds less new practice than its page count suggests; the dropped 2B/6B/7B lessons of the first edition (a reading, a speaking and a writing lesson per unit) have no replacement here.
- This edition's lesson selection does **not** match the Grade 11 Semester 1 Teacher's Book (2024), whose notes teach the first edition's 1B–7B lessons; teachers using this workbook with that Teacher's Book will find notes for lessons that no longer exist and no notes for the inserted Revision spreads beyond the Revision note.
- Watermarked review copy; no audio embedded; only the Revision sections are keyed in the book.

# Jordan — English Grade 11 Jordan High Note, Grade 11: Workbook, Semester 1 (first edition, 72 pp., file "…-9667") — FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-03 (anchors 19/19)

## Workbook-comparison verdict (this file vs the 52-page Grade 11 Semester 1 Workbook)
**Verdict: DIFFERENT EDITIONS of the same book — not the same book, and not different books.** This 72-page file is the **First edition (trial), 2024** Workbook; the 52-page file is the **Second Edition, Revised and Updated, 2025**. Evidence, from the books themselves:
1. **Imprints:** this book — © Pearson/York Press 2024, ISBN **978-9923-41-619-8**, National Library deposit **2024/4/2423**, "First edition (trial) 2024/1445", adopted for 2024/2025. Its ISBN/deposit sit in the original sequential set (Student's Book …618-1 / deposit 2422; this Workbook …619-8 / 2423; Teacher's Book …620-4 / 2424). The 52-page book — © 2025 second edition, ISBN **978-9923-41-924-3**, deposit **2025/3/1621**, "Second Edition, Revised and Updated", adopted for 2025/2026, "Printed 2024, Reprinted 2025–2026".
2. **Structure (contents pages compared):** this edition gives every unit **Lessons 1B–7B** (9 pages per unit) and closes with *Unit Vocabulary Practice* + *Self-check* pages gathered at the back (pp.58–67) and a *Self-check Answer Key* (p.68). The second edition cuts each unit to 4–5 selected B lessons, drops the 2B reading, 6B speaking and 7B writing lessons, deletes the back-of-book vocabulary practice/self-check block, and instead inserts the Student's Book's *Revision* spreads into each unit with a *Revision Answer Key*.
3. **Sampled page-by-page diff across the whole book:** shared lessons are textually identical where both editions keep them (e.g. Lesson 1B "Present and past tenses" opens with the same "Match sentences 1–11" task in both; Lessons 3B idioms and 4B question tags are identical). Where they differ, they differ by deletion and light rewriting, not by new syllabus: Lesson 5B in this edition is *Listening and Vocabulary* (radio interview about language, track 1.9), while the second edition replaces it with *Vocabulary extension + Active Pronunciation*; the Phrasal Verbs appendix examples are reworded between editions (this edition: a short example about an actress's performance / a father picking up a new electric car; second edition: different example sentences for the same headwords).
4. **Teacher's Book alignment:** the Grade 11 Semester 1 Teacher's Book (2024) contains notes for Lessons **2B, 6B and 7B (WB)** — lessons that exist only in this 72-page edition. This is the workbook the Teacher's Book teaches from.
5. **Physical:** 72 PDF pages / 11,329,585 bytes / smaller page size (612×859 pts) vs 52 pages / 12,684,917 bytes / 624×870 pts for the second edition. Both are by Daniel Brayshaw; the second edition's cover committee is restyled "Adaptation Committee in Jordan" and its publisher line adds "and Evaluation".

## The book itself
- Verified from rendered cover/title/imprint pages: **Jordan High Note, Grade 11, Semester 1, Workbook** — First edition (trial). Publisher: National Center for Curriculum Development, Amman; © Pearson Education Limited and York Press Ltd. 2024.
- Author (title page): Daniel Brayshaw. Evaluation and Adaptation Committee: Prof. Nayel Darweesh Al-Shara'h (Head), Prof. Jihad Mohammad Hamdan, Prof. Abdullah Ahmad Bani Abdelrahman, Dr. Mania Moayad Mubaslat. Academic arbitration: Prof. Thafer Y. Assaraira. Active Pronunciation sections by Halina Lewandowska (acknowledgements).
- ISBN 978-9923-41-619-8; deposit 2024/4/2423; adopted for 2024/2025 (NCCD meeting 3/2024, 7/5/2024).
- File: `grade-11_gov_jordan_english-language_student_term-1-workbook-9667.pdf` — 11,329,585 bytes; 72 PDF pages. Printed folios 1–68, then two blank leaves (PDF pp.71–72, verified by rendering — plain white, no content). **Printed page = PDF page − 2**. Read whole via `pdftotext -layout`; 19/19 citation anchors passed.
- Format: star-rated exercises (★–★★★); a full seven B lessons per unit mirroring the Student's Book's seven A lessons; Self-assessment per unit; end-of-book Unit Vocabulary Practice + Self-check per unit with a Self-check Answer Key (the only key printed in the book).

## Full unit/lesson map
- **Unit 1 Get the message (pp.4–13).** 1B Present and past tenses (pp.4–5); 2B Reading and Vocabulary — *Communicating with…*, article on forms of written communication incl. Easter Island's Rongorongo script (pp.6–7); 3B Vocabulary — communication idioms (p.8); 4B Grammar — Question tags and echo questions (p.9); 5B Listening and Vocabulary — radio interview about language (p.10); 6B Speaking — emotion phrases (p.11); 7B Writing — analytical essay: "Is social media not suitable for older people?" with a model essay (p.12). Self-assessment p.23.
- **Unit 3 Influences (pp.24–33).** 1B Past and present habits (pp.24–25); 2B Listening and Vocabulary — radio programme about the influence of books (p.26); 3B Vocabulary — Personal qualities and behaviour (p.27); 4B Reading and Vocabulary — newspaper article on Dickens and the poor (pp.28–29); 5B Grammar — Relative and participle clauses (p.30); 6B Speaking (p.31); 7B Writing — opinion essay (p.32). Self-assessment p.33.
- **Unit 4 Inside story (pp.34–43).** 1B Narrative tenses (pp.34–35); 2B Vocabulary — News reporting (clickbait, viral videos) (p.36); 3B Grammar — Negative inversion (p.37); 4B Reading and Vocabulary — web posts for and against radio (Reem; Manal, 15) (pp.38–39); 5B Listening and Vocabulary — radio phone-in (p.40); 6B Speaking (p.41); 7B Writing — story "Missing in the Desert" (Fadi vanishes; Hamed) (p.42). Self-assessment p.43.
- **Unit 5 Making sense of the senses (pp.44–53).** 1B Gerunds and infinitives (pp.44–45); 2B Vocabulary — The senses (smell adjectives) (p.46); 3B Grammar — Verbs with gerunds and infinitives (p.47); 4B Reading and Vocabulary — article on genetic eye conditions and synaesthesia (pp.48–49); 5B Listening and Vocabulary — radio show (marmalade ingredients) (p.50); 6B Speaking (p.51); 7B Writing — review of a multi-sensory cinema by Jameela Hakim (p.52). Self-assessment p.53.
- **Appendices:** Phrasal Verbs p.54; Prepositions pp.55–56; Word Building + Pronunciation Table p.57; Unit Vocabulary Practice pp.58, 60, 62, 64, 66; Self-check pp.59, 61, 63, 65, 67; Self-check Answer Key p.68.

## Grammar and vocabulary spine for the book
The full Student's Book spine with a dedicated practice lesson for **every** A lesson, including the reading, speaking and writing lessons the second edition later dropped: tenses review + question tags; future forms; habits + relative/participle clauses; narrative tenses + negative inversion; gerunds/infinitives.

## Honest negatives
- Two trailing PDF pages (71–72) are blank — the printed book ends at p.68 (answer key); the blanks are padding in this scan/file, not missing content.
- Only the Self-checks have a printed key; the 1B–7B exercises are keyed in the Teacher's Book, not here.
- Because a same-titled second edition exists, any citation to "the Grade 11 Semester 1 Workbook" is ambiguous unless the edition/ISBN is stated — this filing is for ISBN 978-9923-41-619-8 only.

# Jordan — English Grade 11 Jordan High Note, Grade 11: Teacher's Book, Semester 1 — FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-03 (anchors 18/18)

## The book itself
- Verified from rendered cover/title/imprint pages: **Jordan High Note, Grade 11, Semester 1, Teacher's Book**. Publisher: National Center for Curriculum Development, Amman; © Pearson Education Limited and York Press Ltd. 2024; adopted for 2024/2025.
- Author (title page): Lisa Darrand. Evaluation and Adaptation Committee: Prof. Nayel Darweesh Al-Shara'h (Head), Prof. Jihad Mohammad Hamdan, Prof. Abdullah Ahmad Bani Abdelrahman, Dr. Mania Moayad Mubaslat.
- ISBN 978-9923-41-620-4; National Library deposit 2024/4/2424 (completing the sequential first-edition set: Student's Book 2422, Workbook 2423, Teacher's Book 2424); First edition (trial) 2024/1445.
- File: `grade-11_gov_jordan_english-language_teacher_term-1.pdf` — 8,871,898 bytes; 200 PDF pages. Printed folios to 198 plus a closing NOTES page; **printed page = PDF page − 2** in the notes body (the photocopiable section carries the original international edition's own folio/lesson numbering — see Honest negatives). Read whole via `pdftotext -layout`; 18/18 citation anchors passed.

## What the guide adds (scripts, keys, method)
- **Introduction (printed pp.4–19):** course rationale and methodology (Active Reading/Listening, Watch Out!, Think Back), key concepts, course components, unit walkthrough, teaching pathways, **how to teach for exams** (the book is explicit that Jordan High Note serves exam and non-exam students, and routes exam preparation through the Revision/Use of English strands and self-study resources), and flipped-classroom guidance.
- **Teacher's Notes (pp.20–149):** a Mapping page per unit (scope and sequence, learning outcomes, performance indicators), then notes for **every** Student's Book (A) lesson and **every Workbook (B) lesson of the first-edition 72-page Workbook** — including the 2B reading, 6B speaking and 7B writing lessons (e.g. "LESSON 6B SPEAKING (WB)" at printed p.146, "LESSON 7B WRITING (WB)" at p.148) — plus the unit Revision lessons. Each note: Overview/objectives, staged procedure with minute timings and interaction codes (T–S, S–S, S–T), **answers and suggested answers inline**, **full audioscripts at the point of use** (e.g. Audioscript 5.15 for the Unit 5 Workbook speaking lesson), contingency plans, homework.
- **Culture Spot notes (p.150)** and **Literature Spot notes (p.152)** — including, for *Wuthering Heights*, plot/background notes (the Thrushcross Grange succession, Heathcliff's disappearance) that go beyond the Student's Book extracts (filed here as summary only).
- **Culture Notes (pp.154–157):** background briefings (UN headquarters and official languages; the US "baby boom" 1946–1964; a Shakespeare biography note for the Culture Spot).
- **Class Debates (p.157)** — five-step debate method; **Debating useful language (p.158)**.
- **Photocopiable Resources 1–23 (pp.159–190):** pair/group worksheets with their own teacher's notes and keys — e.g. R1 "Snap is a tense game!" (present/past tenses), R3 "Nice to meet you" (communication idioms), R4 "Let's play tag!" (question tags), R5 "What's your EQ?" (listening), R6 "2050" (future predictions), R8 "Climate change myths and facts", R10 "Would I lie to you?" (habits), R13 "It's all relative" (relative/participle clauses), R14 "We never went to the moon?" (narrative tenses), R16 "Little did I know…" (negative inversion), R19 "Pelmanism" (verb patterns), R22 "The longest 200 metres" (inference), R23 "The Taste Podcast".
- **Graphic Organisers 1–5 (pp.192–199):** planners for the analytical essay, formal email, opinion essay, story and review. Final page: blank NOTES.

## Full unit/lesson map
Units 1–5 exactly as the Student's Book (g11-student-term-1.md): Get the message; Looking ahead; Influences; Inside story; Making sense of the senses — plus Life Skills notes (persuasive presentation, debating). The guide adds mapping, procedure, scripts, keys, culture background and the photocopiable/organiser apparatus; it adds no new language syllabus.

## Grammar and vocabulary spine for the book
Same as the Student's Book (tense review → future forms → habits/clauses → narrative tenses/inversion → gerunds/infinitives). The guide's own spine is exam-and-outcomes mapping: every lesson is tied to curriculum learning outcomes, and the exam section explains how the Revision/Use of English material converts the general course into Tawjihi-style preparation.

## Honest negatives
- **Misprinted divider:** the Photocopiable Resources divider page (PDF p.161) reads "Jordan — **Grade 9** — Photocopiable Resources" — the wrong grade, carried over from another level's Teacher's Book.
- The photocopiable worksheets keep the **original international High Note lesson codes** (1A, 1B, 1C, 1D, 1E, 2F…) and their own folio numbers, which do not match the Jordanian A/B lesson naming used in the notes — teachers must cross-map them via the contents table.
- This Teacher's Book (2024) teaches the **first-edition** Workbook's lessons; it does not match the 2025 second-edition Workbook (see g11-workbook-term-1.md) — there is no Teacher's Book in this set for the second-edition Workbook.
- No separate test bank or end-of-book key/script appendix; keys and scripts are inline only.

- Structure note: unlike Semester 1, this book has **no per-unit Revision/Use of English spreads** — units run straight 1A–7A, with Life Skills after Units 6, 8 and 10, and a Grammar Reference (no "and Practice") at the back.

## Full unit/lesson map
- **Unit 6 Where we live (pp.4–13).** Grammar: modal and related verbs (1A, pp.4–5); articles (3A, p.7). Vocabulary: comparing/contrasting language, nomadic lifestyle (2A, p.6), houses and space (4A, p.8), household problems and solutions (5A, p.10); Active Vocabulary: phrasal verbs. Reading pp.8–9: *Living small* — how much living space we really need; tiny/mobile homes (summary). Listening p.6: radio programme about the mobile lifestyle (Hani on life on the move). Speaking p.11: giving instructions. Writing pp.12–13: a report.
- **Life Skills (pp.14–15): How to make the most of volunteering** (incl. a Giant Beach Clean-up Day).
- **Unit 7 Is it fair? (pp.16–25).** Grammar: reported speech (1A, pp.16–17); reporting verbs (5A, p.22). Vocabulary: charity (pp.16–17), social issues (2A, p.18), kindness (4A, pp.20–21); Active Vocabulary: word building; pronunciation: stress in word families. Reading pp.20–21: *Moving Mountains* (a teenager's sight of Kilimanjaro over the Serengeti leads to action — summary) alongside *Kindness is as Easy as Pie* and *The Milk of Human Kindness*. Listening p.19: documentary films about social issues. Speaking p.23: expressing and challenging opinions. Writing pp.24–25: an article (model: "Happy hyenas" — rehabilitating hyenas' image).
- **Unit 8 Digital perspectives (pp.26–35).** Grammar: the passive, incl. infinitive and gerund forms (1A, pp.26–27); impersonal passive structures (6A, p.33). Vocabulary: the Internet of Things (pp.26–27), technology and synonyms (3A, p.29), virtual reality (4A, pp.30–31); Active Vocabulary: synonyms. Reading pp.30–31: *Virtual Reality: Walking in someone else's shoes* — VR as an empathy tool (summary). Listening p.32: radio programme about selfies. Speaking p.28: describing trends. Writing pp.34–35: a for-and-against essay (how much personal information we give away); Active Writing: evaluating others' views.
- **Life Skills (pp.36–37): How best to use social media.**
- **Unit 9 Highs and lows (pp.38–47).** Grammar: conditionals review and mixed conditionals (1A, pp.38–39); *wish/if only* and past modals (5A, p.44). Vocabulary: ups-and-downs stories (pp.38–39), success and failure (3A, p.40), chance and risk (p.41); Active Vocabulary: binomials. Reading pp.42–43: *Inspirational stories of resilience* — including Aldi Adilang, the Indonesian teenager who survived adrift at sea (summary); Active Reading: following events in a narrative. Listening p.40: personal stories about success and failure. Speaking p.45: discussing advantages and disadvantages. Writing pp.46–47: a competition entry / letter of application ("Win a dream job and travel the world").
- **Unit 10 Culture vulture (pp.48–57).** Grammar: past modals of speculation (1A, pp.48–49); reduced adverbial (participle) clauses (3A, p.51). Vocabulary: ancient artefacts (pp.48–49 — incl. the stone spheres of Costa Rica in the Communication appendix), Jordanian embroidery (2A, p.50), spoilers (4A, pp.52–53), performance (5A, p.54); Active Vocabulary: easily confused words. Reading pp.52–53: *Spoilers: love 'em or hate 'em?* — University of California research suggesting spoilers can make stories more enjoyable (summary; the study is credited to Andy Murdock, UC Newsroom, in the acknowledgements). Listening p.50: radio programme about Jordanian embroidery. Speaking p.55: negotiating informally. Writing pp.56–57: an article.
- **Life Skills (pp.58–59): How to build resilience** — skills and strategies for stressful times.
- **Culture Spot (pp.60–61): Jordan's Parliament** — "everything you need to know" about Jordan's parliament, with a listening conversation (Jordan has 138 MPs).
- **Literature Spot (pp.62–63): Robert Frost, "Stopping by Woods on a Snowy Evening"** — the poem is printed and studied; filed as summary only: a traveller on the darkest evening of the year pauses by snowy woods, his horse puzzled, before remembering his "promises to keep" and travelling on.
- **Back matter:** Word Lists pp.64–68; Grammar Reference pp.69–74; Irregular Verbs p.75; Communication pp.76–79; Acknowledgements p.80.

## Grammar and vocabulary spine for the book
Modals + articles → reported speech + reporting verbs → passive family incl. impersonal passive → conditionals consolidated + mixed conditionals, *wish*/past modals → past modals of speculation + reduced adverbial clauses. Vocabulary: homes/lifestyles, fairness/charity, digital technology, success/failure/risk, culture/heritage/performance. Jordanian content peaks here: Dana (Grade 10), Jordan Museum (Semester 1), Jordanian embroidery and Jordan's Parliament (this book).

## Honest negatives
- No in-book Revision or Use of English exam practice in this semester (Semester 1 has both) — exam practice for Units 6–10 lives in the Workbook's Revision spreads instead.
- The contents page's back-matter line ("Grammar Reference", pp.69–74) matches the book, but one interior header (PDF p.62) reads "Grammar Reference and Practice" over the Culture Spot page — a layout artefact of the two-column opening, not extra content.
- Two trailing PDF pages are blank; no answer key or audio inside the PDF.

## Honest negatives
- Same contents simplification as elsewhere: every unit is labelled "Lessons 1B–5B", but Unit 8 actually runs to 6B and the included lesson sets vary (U6 = 1B/2B/3B/5B; U7 = 1B/2B/3B/5B; U8 = 1B/3B/5B/6B; U9 = 1B/2B/3B/5B; U10 = 1B/2B/3B/5B).
- Appendix continuity with Semester 1: the Phrasal Verbs glossary here uses the first-edition example sentences (e.g. the actress-performance example also found in the 72-page Semester 1 workbook), not the reworded examples of the second-edition Semester 1 workbook — the Semester 2 workbook was never revised in the same way.
- No audio embedded; main-lesson answers only in the Teacher's Book.
